 (i)

INTRODUCTION
I,  the  Chairman  of  the  Department-related  Parliamentary  Standing  Committee  on
Education,  Women,  Children,  Youth  and  Sports , having  been  authorised  by  the  Committee  to
present  the  Report  on  its  behalf,  do  hereby  present  this  Three  Hundred  and  Seventy  Eigh th
Report  of  the  Committee  on  the  D e mands  for  Grants  (2026-27)  (Demand  No.  10 2 ) pertaining
to  the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports .
2. In  accordance  with  the  constitutional  requirement  under  Article  113,  the  estimated
expenditure  of  the  Ministries/Departments  of  the  Government  of  India  projected  under  various
Demands  for  Grants  for  the  upcoming  financial  year  needs  to  be  voted  by  Parliament.  As  a
sequel  thereto,  Demands  for  Grants  of  the  relevant  Ministries/Departments  stand  referred  to
concerned  Department-related  Parliamentary  Standing  Committee  to  make  a  close  scrutiny
thereof  under  Rule  270  of  R ules  of  Procedure  and  Conduct  of  Business  in  the  Council  of  States
(Rajya  Sabha) . While  making  scrutiny  of  the  Demands  for  Grants,  the  Committee  has  made  an
appraisal  of  the  performance,  programmes , schemes  and  policies  of  the  Department  of  Higher
Education  vis-a-vis  expenditure  made  out  of  Consolidated  Fund  of  India  in  the  current  financial
year.
3 . In  this  Report,  a  scrutiny  of  Demand  No.102  pertaining  to  the  Department  of  Youth
Affairs  and  Department  of  Sports  of  the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Sports  for  the  Financial
Year 2026-27 has been made by the Committee.
4.  During  the  course  of  examination  of  Demands,  the  Committee  heard  the  views  of  the
Secretary,  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Department  of  Sports  along  with  representatives
of  the  Nehru  Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan  (NYKS),  National  Service  Scheme  (NSS);  Directors-
General  of             the  Sports  Authority  of  India  (SAI)  and  National  Anti-Doping  Agency  (NADA)
and  Vice-Chancellor  of  the                                 National  S p o rts U n iv e rsity  in  its  meetings  held  on  2 0 th  February,
202 6 .
5 . The  Committee,  while  making  its  observations/recommendations,  has  mainly  relied
upon the following  documents :-
(i) Detailed Demands for Grants ( 2026 -2 7 );
(ii) Annual Report for the year 202 4 -2 5 ;
(iii) Annual Plan for the year 2026 -2 7 ;
(iv) Appropri ation Accounts for the year 2025-26 ;
(v) Detailed  Explanatory  Note  (Demand-wise)  on  the  Demands  for  Grants  (2026 -2 7 )
pertaining  to  the  Department  and  Organizations/Institutes  under  its  administrative
control  along  with  a  brief  synopsis  of  the  Scheme/Item  for  which  Demand  is
being raised; 
(vi) Output  Outcome  Monitoring  Framework  (OOMF)  2026 -2 7 , relating  to  all
Schemes under the Department;
(vii) Written  replies  furnished  by  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Department  of
Sports  respectively  on  the  Questionnaires  (Issues  and  Schemes  &  Budgetary
Allocations)  prepared by  the Secretariat;
(viii) Written  clarifications  furnished  by  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and
Department  of  Sports  respectively  to  the  points/issues  raised  by  Members  in  the
a  b  o  v  e    s  a  i d    meeting s of the Committee.
(ii)

(ix) Latest available Annual/Administrative Re por t(s) of the Organization(s) etc; and
(x) Presentations  made  by  the  Secretary  of  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and
Department  of  Sports  of  the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Sports  covering
Nehru  Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan  (NYKS),  National  Service  Scheme  (NSS),  Sports
Authority  of  India  (SAI),  National  Anti-Doping  Agency  (NADA)  and  N  a  t i o  n  a  l 
S  p  o  r t s   U  n  i v  e  r s  i t y   under  the  Administrative  Control  of  the  Ministry  of  Youth
Affairs  and Sports.
6 . The  Report  is  based  on  facts,  figures  and  submissions  (both  oral  and  written)  tendered
by  Departments  and  Organisations  under  the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Sports,  to  the
Committee .
7 . The  Committee  wishes  to  express  its  thanks  to  the  Secretary  and  officers  of  the
Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and  the  Department  of  Sports  as  well  as  Heads/Officers  of  the
organizations/agencies  under  the  administrative  control  of  the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and
Sports  for  appearing  before  the  Committee  and  furnishing  the  requisite  information  in
connection   with  the examination of   the Demands for Grants  of  the Ministry.
8 . The  Committee  considered  and  adopted  the  Report  at  its  mee ting  held  on  16 th  March ,
202 6  and presented to both Houses of Parliament on 18 th  March, 202 6 .
9. For  the  facility  of  reference  and  convenience,  the  observations  and  recommendations  of
the   Committee  have been printed  in bold  letters  in the body of the  Report.

1
REPORT
Department of Youth Affairs
1. INTRODUCTION
 1.1 India  has  the  largest  youth  population  in  the  world.  To  optimally  tap  their  constructive  and
creative  energies,  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  (DoYA ) pursues  the  twin  objectives  of
personality  - development  and  nation  building  that  is,  developing  the  personality  of  youth  and
involving  them  in  various  nation-building  activities  constructive  leadership  skills  to  promote  the
spirit  of  community  service  through  its  various  organisations'.  They  participate  in  various  initiatives
and  provide  support  to  all  Ministries,  bringing  in  Janbhagidari -se- Janvishwas .  All  organisations
work  through  both  their  field  presence  and  My  Bharat  portal.  The  objective  of  this  entire
organisation  is  to  provide  exposure  to  youth  in  diverse  work  settings,  cutting  across  departmental
silos,  so  as  to  facilitate  building  vast  youth  network.   The  Department  has  also  recognized
"Adolescents"  as  an  important  segment  of  the  Youth.  The  role  of  the  DoYA  is  to  act  as  a  facilitator
and  catalytic  agent  for  youth  development.  Since  youth  is  a  very  diverse  category,  the  Department
of  Youth  Affairs  synergies  their  efforts  with  multiple  Departments  like  Education,  Health  and
Family  Welfare  etc.  to  cater  to  the  interests  of  youth.  The  State  Governments  and  a  number  of  other
stakeholders  are  also  working  to  support  youth  development  and  to  enable  productive  youth
participation.  The  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  &  Sports  was  bi-furcated  into  two  Departments  in
April, 2008, namely, Department of Youth Affairs and Department of Sports.
1.2 The  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  formulated  the  National  Youth  Policy  (NYP),  2014,
which  proposes  a  holistic  ‘vision ’ for  the  youth  of  India  and  “ To  empower  youth  of  the  country  to
achieve  their  full  potential,  and  through  them  enable  India  to  find  its  rightful  place  in  the
community  of  nations".In  order  to  realize  this  Vision,  the  NYP-2014  identifies  5  clearly  defined
‘Objectives ’ which  need  to  be  pursued  and  the  ‘Priority  Areas ’ under  each  of  the  Objectives.  The
objectives and priority areas identified under the NYP-2014 are summarized below:
TABLE-1
Objectives Priority Areas
1. Create  a  productive  workforce  that  can
make  a  sustainable  contribution  to  India ’s
economic development
1. Education
2. Employment and skill development
3. Entrepreneurship
2. Develop  a  strong  and  healthy  generation
equipped to take on future challenges
4. Health and healthy lifestyle
5. Sports
3. Instill  social  values  and  promote
community service to build national ownership
6. Promotion of social values
7. Community engagement
4. Facilitate  participation  and  civic
engagement at all levels of governance
8. Participation in politics and governance
9. Youth engagement
5. Support  youth  at  risk  and  create  equitable
opportunity  for  all  dis -advantaged  and
marginalised  youth
10. Inclusion
1.3 Vision  of  the  DoYA  is  as  under:  To  act  as  a  key  enabler  in  the  process  of  all-round
development  and  empowerment  of  Youth  of  the  country,  to  enable  them  to  realize  their  full
potential  and  thereby  contribute  to  the  nation-building  process.  to  develop  strong  and  healthy

2
generations.  to  instill  social  values  and  promote  community  service,  facilitate  participation  and
civic engagement, support youth at risk and create equitable opportunity for all.
1.4 Mission:
 To  formulate  policies  aimed  at  promotion  and  development  of  Youth  of  the
Country.
 To  formulate  and  implement  policies  and  programmes  aimed  at  facilitating  the  all
round development of the personality of youth through community service.
 To  formulate  and  implement  policies  and  programmes  aimed  at  involving  the  youth
in various nation-building activities.
 Strengthen engagement programmes for youth.
 Support organizations working towards spreading social values and harmony
 Leverage community development organization
 Engage youth outside the political system
 To  leverage  the  large  number  of  youth  organizations   toward  community
development, to generate positive outcomes at the grassroots level
 Promote volunteerism by involving youth in community development activities.
1.5 The Schemes / initiatives implemented by Department of Youth Affairs as on under :
1.5.1 Rashtriya  Yuva  Sashaktikaran  Karyakram  (RYSK):  This  is  an  umbrella  Scheme  with  the
following components/ sub-schemes, namely, 
(i) MY Bharat [erstwhile Nehru Yuva Kendra Sangathan (NYKS)]
(ii) National Youth Corps
(iii) National Young Leaders Programme
(iv) National  Programme for Youth and Adolescent Development
(v) International Cooperation
(vi) Youth Hostels
(vii) Assistance to Scouting and Guiding Organisation
1.5.2 National Service Scheme (NSS)
1.5.3. Rajiv Gandhi National Institute of Youth Development (RGNIYD) .

2 OVERALL  ASSESSMENT  OF  THE  DEMANDS  FOR  GRANTS  (2026-27)  OF
THE DEPARTMENT OF YOUTH AFFAIRS
2.1 The  Committee  considered  the  Demands  for  Grants  (2026-27)  of  the  Department  of
Youth  Affairs  in  its  meeting  held  on  20 th  February  2026.  The  Demand  for  Grants  (2026-27)  of
the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Sports  has  been  presented  to  Parliament  under  the  following
Demand Numbers.:
Demand No. 102-  Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports
2.2 This  Report  pertains  to  the  Demands  for  Grants  (Demand  No.  102)  relating  to
Department of Youth Affairs.
Budget Summary:
2.3 The  outlay  under  BE  2026-27  of  Ministry  of  Youth  A ﬀ airs  and  Sports  is
₹ 4479.88  crore s  and  its  share  in  the  total  budgetary  allocation  of  ₹  53 ,47,315  crores
for  FY  2026-27  of  the  Govt.  of  India  is  0.084  percent  out  of  the  total  budgetary
allocation  for  the  financial  year  2026-27  in  respect  of  Ministry  of  Youth  A ﬀ airs  and
Sports  i.e.  ₹ 4479.88  crores ,  an  amount  of  ₹ 1366.52  crores .  has  been  allocated  to
Department  of  Youth  A ﬀ airs ,  which  is  30.50  per  cent  of  the  total  budget  allocation
by the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports.
2.4  The  amount  under  Revenue  Head  has  increased  to  Rs.  4461.51  crores  in  BE  2026-27  for
the  Ministry  from  Rs.  3343.43  crores  allocated  in  RE  2025-26.  Out  o f the  allocation  of  Rs.
4461.51c rores,  the  allocation  for  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  under  Revenue  Head  in  BE
2026-27  is  1349.60  c rores.  These  variations  are  due  to  the  fact  that  additional  fund  required
under the following schemes:
(a)  NSS  Scheme  to  increase  the  enrolment  of  NSS  volunteers  (25,000  units,  1  unit  =  100
volunteers 
(b) Yuva Portal for revamping and monitoring of MY Bharat, training expenditure,
(c)  International  Cooperation  scheme  for  proposed  program  Global  Outreach:
Deployment of Indian Youth Volunteers, 
(d) Assistance to National Sports Federations scheme for infra projects
(e ) Khelo India Scheme for infra project
(f)  Promotion  of  Sports  Goods  Manufacturing,  a  newly  added  scheme  under  Department
of Sports.
2.5 During  the  course  of  the  meeting,  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  has  also  submitted  the
details  of  the  figures  of  projection,  allocation,  utilization  and  variations.  The  variation  in  percentage
between  BE  2025-26  and  BE  2026-27  is  7.2%  while  the  variation  in  percentage  between  RE  2025-
26 and BE 2026-27 stands at 30.50%.

2.9 From  figures  given  at  Table  3,  it  is  evident  that  Rs.  1,273.77  crores  has  been
allocated  under  BE  2025-26  which  has  been  decreased  to  Rs.999.40  crores  at  RE  stage.  The
Committee  observes  that  this  reduction  at  RE  stage  amounts  to  Rs.274.37  crores  which  is
nearly  21.54%  less  in  comparison  to  allocation  made  at  BE  2025-26.  Further,  the
Committee  notes  that  the  Actual  expenditure  of  the  Department  is  only  Rs.  696.68  crores  as
on  31.01.2026  which  is  only  69.71%  of  the  reduced  allocations  of  RE  2025-26.  In  this
regard,  the  Committee  would  like  to  recommend  the  Department  to  utilize  funds  timely,  so
that, no rush for expenditure particularly in the closing quarter of financial year.

2.19 Under  BE  and  RE  for  2025-26  for  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  and  Sports,  the  allocated  fund
under  the  head  “ Secretariat  –  DoYA ” , is  Rs.  41.79  Crores  and  51.68  crores,  respectively;  BE  2026-
27  is  Rs.  33.78  Crores  for  Department  of  Youth  Affairs.  During  the  course  of  the  meeting,  the
Committee  was  informed  that  the  reason  for  the  variation  of  BE  2026-27  when  compared  to  BE
2025-26  is  due  to  the  reasons  that  the  Establishment  expenditure  of  both  the  Departments  i.e.
Department  of  Sports  and  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  has  been  booked  under  the  Department
of  Youth  Affairs.  From  BE  2026-27  both  the  Departments  have  separate  Secretariat  funds.  An
Allocation  of  Rs.31.85  crores  in  New  Financial  Year  (NFY)  BE  2026-27  has  been  made  under
establishment  expenditure  of  Department  of  Sports.   The  reason  for  increased  BE  2026-27  is  due
to expansion of Ministry's sanctioned strength and posting of more staff.
2.20 Further,  under  scheme/autonomous/sub-ordinate  office  head,  the  Department  has  apprised
the  Committee  that  reasons  for  variations  in  BE  2026-27  compared  to  BE  2025-26  is  due  to
expansion  of  schemes  of  the  Department,  proposed  enhancement  of  units  and  staff  strength  of  My
Bharat- NSS,  higher  outgo  on  volunteer  honorarium,  training  and  administration,  Ministry-
organized  VBYLD  events,  increased  youth  hostel  renovation  proposals,  etc.   Further,  variation  in

9
BE  2026-27  vis-à-vis  RE  2025-26  have  been  enhanced  due  to  proposed  expansion  of  schemes.
With  potential  expansion  of  schemes  and  initiatives  and  required  sanctioned  strength  being  more
than  existing  strength,  more  staff  is  likely  to  be  posted.  Accordingly,  MY  Bharat- NSS  has  sought
additional  allocations  for  addition  of  new  units.  The  volunteer  base  under  National  Youth  Corps
(NYC)  scheme  is  under  expansion  and  proposal  for  enhanced  honorarium  is  under  consideration  in
the  Department;  conducting  of  National  Youth  Festival  in  revised  format  of  Vikshit  Bharat  Young
Leaders  Dialogue  (VBYLD)  and  enhanced  participation  and  increased  scope  of  National  Youth
Parliament  festival;  re-structuring  of  Youth  Hostel  Scheme  and  funds  requirement  for  development
and  maintenance  of  MY  Bharat  web  portal  including  IT-Project  Monitoring  Unit  (PMU).  It  was
also  informed  that  the  newly  created  Autonomous  Body  Mera  Yuva  Bharat  is  also  functional  now
and increased program activity is expected.

2.22 The  Committee  takes  note  of  the  vacancies  in  Secretariat- DoYA  and  observes  that  61
posts  are  vacant  against  the  sanctioned  strength  240.  The  Committee  also  observes  that  4 ,32

therefore,  recommends  the  Department  to  complete  the  process  of  filling  up  the  vacancies  in  a
time-bound  manner  and  latest  by  the  end  of  2026,  so  that,  implementation  of  schemes  and
programmes  of  the  Department  does  not  suffer  and  its  benefits  reach  down  to  the  grass  root

3. Schemes/initiatives implemented by DoYA
3.1. Rashtriya Yuva Sashaktikaran Karyakram:
Rashtriya  Yuva  Sashaktikaran  Karyakram  (RYSK)  is  a  flagship  scheme  of  the  Department  of
Youth  Affairs  that  subsumes  seven  existing  schemes  for  youth  empowerment  for  achieving  better
synergies  between  the  Schemes  and  thereby,  improve  their  effectiveness  and  help  in  achieving
better  outcomes  with  the  available  resources.  The  details  of  BE  2025-26,  RE  2025-26,  Projections
to  Ministry  of  Finance  for  BE  2026-27  and  BE  2026-27  of  the  sub  schemes  under  RYSK  are  as
follows:-

3.2 The variation in percentage of BE  2026-27 over RE 2025-26 stands at 82.37%. When BE
2026-27 is compared to the projections of the MoF, it stands at -12.05%, while the variation of 
percentage between BE  2026-27 and BE 2025-26 stands at 17.04%.
3.2.1   National  Youth  Corps  Scheme:  NYC  is  a  sub-scheme  under  the  umbrella  scheme  Rashtriya
Yuva  Sashaktikaran  Karyakram  (RYSK)  of  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  with  MY  Bharat
(erstwhile  NYKS)  serving  as  executing  agency,  which  enables  young  men  and  women  in  the  age
group  of  18-29  years  to  serve  for  a  period  initially  for  one  year  and  extendable  for  one  more  year  on
a  full  time  basis  for  which  they  receive  an  honorarium  of  Rs.  5000/-per  month.  The  main  objective
of  the  scheme  is  to  develop  spirit  of  Volunteerism  and  leadership  qualities  among  the  selected

12
youth.  The  objective  is  to  set  up  a  group  of  disciplined  and  dedicated  youth  who  possess  the
inclination  and  spirit  to  actively  engage  in  nation-building.  These  youth  will  facilitate  the
realization  of  inclusive  social  and  economic  growth,  serve  as  key  points  for  dissemination  of
information  and  basic  knowledge  within  the  community,  and  act  as  group  modulators  and  peer
educators.
3.2.2 In  BE  2025-26  Rs  65.00  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  reduced  to
Rs  22.00  crores.  The  projection  of  MoF  for  BE  2026-27  is  Rs  95.10  crores  and  BE  2026-27
allocation  stands  at  Rs  77.00  crores  .The  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27
over  RE  2025-26  stands  at  250%.  BE  2026-27  over  projections  of  MoF  stands  at  -19.03%.  While
variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  BE  2025-26  stands  at  18.46%.  The
Department  has  informed  that  the  3  volunteers  each  will  be  placed  in  7319  blocks  instead  of
volunteers  in  6600  blocks  earlier.   In  addition,  one  volunteer  as  District  level  coordinator  is
being considered for deployment in each of the 786 districts.
3.2.3 The  sharp  82.37  per  cent  increase  in  BE  2026-27  over  RE  2025-26  indicates  a
substantial  contraction  in  spending  during  the  previous  fiscal  year.  The  Committee  is  of  the
view  that  a  Department  level  audit  may  be  conducted  to  identify  the  grey  areas  that  led  to
the low utilization of RE 2025-26.  
3.2.4 During  the  course  of  the  meeting,  the  Department  has  informed  that  the  sanctioned
strength  for  MY  Bharat  Youth  Volunteers  under  the  NYC  scheme  is  13,206 . However,
targets  for  the  2026-27  financial  year  indicate  a  plan  to  deploy  exactly  this  sanctioned
amount,  suggesting  that  any  existing  gaps  must  be  closed  immediately  to  ensure  full
operational  capacity . The  Committee  is  of  the  view  that  the  Department  should  ensure  100
per  cent  deployment  against  the  sanctioned  strength  of  13,206  volunteers  and  time-bound
recruitment  and  deployment  calendar  should  also  be  strictly  followed  to  prevent  any
service gaps at the block and district levels.
3.2.5 The  Committee  was  apprised  that  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  is  planning  for  a
significant  expansion  of  the  NYC  volunteer  base,  increasing  placement  from  6,600  blocks  to
7,319  blocks  in  the  country . Additionally,  there  is  a  proposal  to  deploy  one  volunteer  as  a
District  Level  Coordinator  in  each  of  the  786  districts . The  Committee,  therefore,
recommends  the  Department  to  prioritize  the  deployment  of  these  District  Level
Coordinators  to  bridge  the  gap  between  block-level  activities  and  district-level
administration  as  this  structure  will  improve  monitoring  and  reporting  of  grassroot  level
initiatives through the MY Bharat platform.
3.2.6 The  Committee  notes  that  under  NYC  volunteers  serve  for  an  initial  period  of  one
year,  extendable  to  a  maximum  of  two  years . As  a  result,  there  is  a  need  to  track  the
professional  and  academic  progress  of  these  volunteers  after  their  term  ends  to  assess  the
long-term  impact  of  the  scheme . The  Committee  recommends  that  in  order  to  leverage  the
existing  MY  Bharat  Portal,  which  already  provides  dedicated  profile  pages  and  digital
credentials  for  youth , the  Department  should  develop  a  alumni  tracking  module  for  NYC

13
volunteers  that  will  help  inform  future  policy  adjustments  and  create  a  valuable  alumni
network for peer mentoring and professional opportunities .
3.3 National Programme for Youth and Adolescent Development:
National  Programme  for  Youth  and  Adolescent  Development  (NPYAD)  is  a  sub-scheme  of  the
Umbrella  Scheme  viz.,  RYSK,  under  which  financial  assistance  is  provided  to  Government/  non-
Government  organisations  for  taking  up  activities  for  youth  and  adolescent  development.  The
Scheme  is  operational  since  1 st  April,  2008.  The  assistance  under  NPYAD  is  provided  under  5
major components, namely,
a) Youth Leadership and Personality Development Training
b) Promotion  of  National  Integration  (National  Integration  Camps,  Inter-State  Youth  Exchange
Programmes, Youth Festivals, multi-cultural activities, etc.)
c) Promotion of Adventure; Tenzing  Norgay National Adventure Awards
d) Development  and  Empowerment  of  Adolescents  (Life  Skills  Education,  Counselling , Career
Guidance, etc.)
e) Technical  and  Resource  Development  (Research  and  Studies  on  Youth  issues,  Documentation,
Seminars/ Workshops)
3.3.1 In  BE  2025-26,  Rs  25.00  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  increased
to  Rs  57.68  crores.  The  projection  of  the  Department  to  Ministry  of  Finance  for  BE  2026-27  was
Rs  58.41  crores  and  the  projected  amount  got  allotted  in  BE  2026-27.The  variations  of  percentage
in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  RE  2025-26  stands  at  1.27%.  While  variations  of  percentage
in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  BE  2025-26  stands  at  133.64%.  The  Department  has  sought
higher  funds  as  the  National  Youth  Festival  has  been  restructured  as  the  Viksit  Bharat  Young
Leaders Dialogue (VBYLD) –  National Youth Festival in a new format.
3.3.2 The  Committee  notes  that  the  Department  recognizes  of  Youth  Affairs
"Adolescents"  (age  10-19)  as  a  distinct  and  vital  subset  of  the  youth  population.  However,
the  budget  for  NPYAD  is  often  grouped  under  broader  youth  leadership  heads.  The
Committee,  therefore,  recommends  that  a  dedicated  sub-component  or  a  ring-fenced
budget  within  NPYAD  specifically  for  adolescent-focused  interventions  should  be  created
and  must  include  life-skills  education  and  counseling  services  to  address  the  unique
psychological  and  physiological  transitions  of  this  age  group,  ensuring  that  they  are  not
overshadowed by the 15-29 age bracket .
3.3.3 The  Committee  was  informed  that  a  restructuring  of  NPYAD  is  underway  to
accommodate  the  Viksit  Bharat  Young  Leaders  Dialogue  (VBYLD),  with  the  budget  for
NPYAD  increasing  to  ₹ 58.41  crores  in  BE  2026-27  (up  from  ₹ 40  crores  in  BE  2025-26).  The
Committee  is  of  the  view  that  as  this  dialogue  is  not  limited  to  State  capitals  only,  given  the
46  per  cent  increase  in  budgetary  allocation,  The  program  should  be  decentralized  to  the
district  level  to  identify  and  mentor  "Change  Makers"  from  rural  and  tribal  backgrounds,
providing them a direct platform to contribute to the vision of a developed India by 2047.

14
3.3.4 The  Committee  recommends  the  Department  to  implement  a  mandatory  minimum
33  per  cent  quota  for  female  participation  in  all  NPYAD-funded  adventure  treks  and
leadership  camps.  Further,  specialized  "All-Women"  leadership  workshops  should  be
introduced  to  encourage  participation  from  regions  where  cultural  barriers  might
otherwise limit mixed-gender engagement.
3.3.5 The  Committee  notes  that  since  NPYAD  aims  at  "Personality  Development,"  the
current  activity  list  is  heavily  weighted  toward  events  and  camps.  The  Committee,
therefore  recommends  Department  that  a  portion  of  the  NPYAD  budget  should  be  utilized
to  provide  small-scale  "Youth  Innovation  Grants"  for  community-based  research  projects.
By  partnering  with  RGNIYD,  the  Department  can  encourage  youth  to  conduct  local
surveys  or  develop  social  innovation  prototypes,  thereby  shifting  the  scheme's  focus  from
mere "participation" to "problem-solving."
3.3.6 The  Committee  notes  that  NPYAD  involves  providing  grants  to  Voluntary
Organizations  (NGOs)  for  youth  development  activities  but  outstanding  Utilization
Certificates  (UCs)  remain  a  major  concern  for  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs.  The
Committee  recommends  to  move  toward  a  100  per  cent  digital  monitoring  system  for
NPYAD  grants.  Future  funding  to  NGOs  should  be  strictly  contingent  upon  the  submission
of  geo-tagged  photos  and  video  evidence  of  the  programs  conducted,  alongside  the  timely
filing  of  UCs  and  will  help  ensure  that  the  increased  outlay  of  ₹ 58.41  crores  in  BE  2026-27
translates into verifiable grassroots impact.
3.4  International Cooperation:
International  Cooperation  Sub-Scheme  is  one  of  the  eight  sub-schemes  of  Rashtriya  Yuva
Sashaktikaran  Karyakram  (RYSK).  The  objectives  of  International  Cooperation  Sub-Scheme  are
endeavors  to  create  an  international  perspective  among  the  youth  in  collaboration  with  other
countries  and  International  Agencies/organizations  on  various  youth  issues  to  involve  them  in
promoting peace and understanding.
To  achieve  this  objective,  the  Department  signs  Memorandum  of  Understandings  (MoUs )
with  various  countries  and  international  organisations , in  consultation  with  Ministry  of  External
Affairs  (MEA),  Government  of  India.  The  aims  of  MoUs  are  to  strengthen  and  encourage
cooperation on youth matters through participation in events and activities such as:
 Exchanges  of  (i)  youth  (ii)  representatives  of  youth  organizations  and  (iii)
government officials in charge of youth policy-making.
 Extension  of  invitation  to  international  conferences  and  seminars  on  youth  matters
organized in different nation(s).
 Participation  in  youth  camps,  youth  festivals  and  other  youth  events  held  in
different nation(s).
 Any other cooperative activities on youth in different nation(s).

15
The  Department  endeavours  to  create  an  international  perspective  among  youth  in
collaboration  with  other  countries  and  international  agencies/  organizations  on  various  youth  issues.
The  Department  also  collaborates  with  UN  Agencies  like  United  Nations  Volunteers  (UNV)/
UNICEF and the Commonwealth Youth Programme (CYP) on various youth related issues.
3.4.1 In  BE  2025-26  Rs  55.00  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  reduced  to
Rs  11.00  crores.  The  projection  of  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  to  Ministry  of  Finance  for  BE
2026-27  was  Rs  55.00  crores  and  BE  2026-27  allocation  stands  at  Rs  35.00  crores.The  variations  of
percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  RE  2025-26  stands  at  218.18%.  BE  2026-27  over
projections  to  MoF  and  percentage  decrease  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  BE  2025-26
stand  at  -36.36%.  The  Committee  was  given  to  understand  that  India  is  hosting  BRICS  in  2026
and  Youth  Track  is  chaired  by  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  and  events  as  defined  have  to
take place during the year for variations.
3.4.2 The  Committee  notes  that  the  Department  has  been  increasingly  involved  in
multilateral  forums  such  as  the  G20  (Y20),  BRICS,  and  SCO.  While  these  platforms  offer
immense  visibility,  the  current  budgetary  allocation  must  reflect  India's  aspiration  to  lead
the  global  youth  agenda.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  to  seek  a  sustained
increase  in  the  "International  Cooperation"  head  at  the  Revised  Estimates  (RE)  stage  for
the  Financial  Year  2026-27,  which  will  ensure  that  India  can  host  high-quality  bilateral  and
multilateral exchanges without compromising on the frequency of delegations.
3.4.3 The  Committee  further  notes  that  the  selection  process  involves  various  tiers  of
screening  for  representation  in  international  youth  delegations.  The  Committee,  therefore,
recommends  that  the  entire  application  and  selection  process  for  International  Youth
Exchange  Programmes  (IYEP)  should  be  fully  integrated  into  the  MY  Bharat  Portal  which
would  provide  a  transparent,  merit-based  automated  shortlisting  system  based  on  the
"digital  credentials"  and  "verified  volunteering  hours"  of  the  youth  and  ensure  that
opportunities reach deserving candidates from remote districts.
3.4.4 The  Committee  has  earlier  emphasized  that  international  visits  should  not  be  "one-
off"  events  but  should  contribute  to  the  national  knowledge  pool.  The  Committee
recommends  that  a  "Post-Visit  Action  Report"  system  is  institutionalised  and  every
returning  delegate  should  be  mandated  to  conduct  at  least  three  "Knowledge  Sharing
Workshops"  in  their  local  NSS  units  or  MY  Bharat  hubs.  Furthermore,  an  annual  impact
assessment  study  should  be  commissioned  through  RGNIYD  to  evaluate  how  these
international  exposures  have  influenced  the  career  trajectories  and  leadership  roles  of  the
participants.
3.4.5 It  is  currently  believed  that  the  International  Cooperation  is  focused  heavily  on
cultural  exchange  and  government-to-government  (G2G)  visits.  The  Committee
recommends  that  the  Department  should  broaden  the  scope  of  International  Cooperation
by  facilitating  Memorandum  of  Understandings  between  RGNIYD  and  leading
international  youth  research  institutes  and  should  focus  on  academic  credits  for  youth
work,  joint  research  on  adolescent  psychology,  and  faculty  exchange,  thereby  upgrading
the domestic academic framework for youth development to global standards.

16
3.5  Youth Hostels:
Youth  Hostels  (YH)  are  built  to  promote  youth  travel  and  to  enable  the  young  people  to
experience  the  rich  cultural  heritage  of  the  country.   The  construction  of  YH  is  a  joint  venture  of  the
Central  and  State  Governments.   While  the  Central  Government  bears  the  cost  of  construction,  the
State  Governments  provide  fully  developed  land  free-of-cost,  with  water  supply,  electricity
connection  and  approach  roads.   YHs  are  located  in  areas  of  historical  and  cultural  value,  in
educational  centres , in  tourist  destinations,  etc.   YHs  provide  good  accommodation  for  the  youth  at
reasonable rates.  
3.5.1 In  BE  2025-26  Rs  5.00  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  reduced  to
Rs  1.10  crores.  The  projection  of  Department  to  Ministry  of  Finance  for  BE  2026-27  and  BE  2026-
27  allocation  stands  at  Rs  19.20  crores.  The  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-
27  over  RE  2025-26  stands  at  1645.45%.  While  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE
2026-27  over  BE  2025-26  stands  at  284%.  The  Department  has  apprised  the  Committee  that
the  Capital  expenditure  will  be  done  for  creation  of  Youth  Hostel  –  Youth  Resource  Hubs  for
Youth of the Country.
3.5.2 The  Committee  observes  that  out  of  the  85  Youth  Hostels  constructed  across  the
country,  several  are  in  a  state  of  disrepair  or  are  underutilized.  While  BE  2026-27  has
allocated  ₹ 19.20  crores  for  Youth  Hostels  (a  significant  increase  from  the  ₹ 1.1  crores  in  RE
2025-26),  the  focus  should  shift  toward  comprehensive  modernization  of  Youth  Hostels.
The  Committee  recommends  that  the  BE  of  ₹ 19.20  crores  outlay  should  be  utilized  for  the
major  repair,  renovation,  and  "facelift"  of  identified  hostels,  particularly  those  in  heritage
or  high-tourist  traffic  areas.  Modernization  should  include  digital  connectivity  (Wi-Fi),
refurbished  dormitories,  and  energy-efficient  infrastructure  to  make  them  appealing  to
contemporary youth travelers.
3.5.3 The  Committee  observes  that  most  of  the  Youth  Hostels  function  merely  as  low-cost
lodging  facilities  and  feels  that  these  spaces  should  serve  a  larger  purpose  in  youth
development  of  the  country.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  that  functional
mandate  of  Youth  Hostels  should  be  redefined  by  transforming  them  into  "Youth  Resource
Hubs."  These  hubs  should  host  career  counseling  centers,  digital  skill-training  workshops,
and  localized  MY  Bharat  registration  desks,  ensuring  the  infrastructure  is  utilized
throughout the day rather than just for overnight stays.
3.5.4 The  Committee  observes  that  a  few  State  Governments  have  diverted  Youth  Hostel
premises  for  administrative  offices  or  other  non-youth-related  purposes.  The  Committee
recommends  that  a  physical  audit  of  all  85  hostels  should  be  conducted  to  find  out  the
current  usage.  A  strict  "Usage  Protocol"  should  be  enforced,  and  any  State  Government
found  utilizing  these  premises  for  non-scheme  purposes  should  be  issued  a  formal  notice  to
restore  the  facility  to  its  original  mandate  and  subsequent  developmental  grants  for  that
state under the RYSK umbrella may be suspended.
3.5.5 The  Committee  also  notes  that  one  of  the  bottlenecks  in  the  popularity  of  Youth
Hostels  is  the  lack  of  a  seamless,  transparent,  and  modern  booking  mechanism.  The
Committee  recommends  the  Department  to  integrate  a  real-time,  centralized  booking
module  within  the  MY  Bharat  Portal  that  would  allow  youth  to  check  availability  and  book

17
rooms  across  the  country  using  their  digital  credentials.  Such  a  system  would  also  provide
the  Department  with  accurate,  real-time  data  on  occupancy  rates  and  revenue  generation,
facilitating better management.
3.6 Assistance  to  Scouting  and  Guiding  Organizations:  The  Department  provides  assistance
to  the  Scouting  and  Guiding  Organizations,  with  a  view  to  promote  the  Scouts  and  Guides
movement  in  the  country.  This  is  an  international  movement  aimed  at  building  character,
confidence,  idealism  and  spirit  of  patriotism  and  service  among  young  boys  and  girls.  Scouting  and
Guiding  also  seeks  to  promote  balanced  physical  and  mental  development  among  the  boys  and
girls.  Financial  assistance  is  provided  to  scouting  and  guiding  organizations  for  various
programmes  such  as  organization  of  training  camps,  skill  development  programmes,  holding  of
jamborees,  activities  related  to  adult  literacy,  environment  conservation,  community  service,  health
awareness and promotion of hygiene and sanitation. 
3.6.1 In  BE  2025-26  Rs  0.75  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  further
reduced  to  Rs  0.50  crores.  The  projection  of  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  to  MoF  for  BE  2026-27
was  Rs.  5  crores  and  BE  2026-27  allocation  stands  at  Rs  3.00  crores.  The  variations  of  percentage
in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  RE  2025-26  stands  at  500%.  BE  2026-27  over  projections  to
MoF  stands  at  -40.00%.  While  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  BE
2025-26  stands  at  300%.  The  Department  has  sought  extra  funds  in  BE  2026-27  for  providing
assistance  to  Scouts  and  Guides  for  various  activities  such  as  training  camps,  skills
development programmes etc.
3.6.2 The  Committee  notes  that  budgetary  provision  for  Scouting  and  Guiding  has
increased  from  ₹ 0.75  crores  in  BE  2025-26  to  ₹ 3.00  crores  in  BE  2026-27.  However,  given
the  vast  organizational  network  and  the  potential  to  reach  millions  of  young  students
across  rural  and  urban  India,  the  current  outlay  remains  relatively  modest  compared  to
other  youth  programs.  The  Committee  recommends  the  Department  to  evaluate  the
possibility  of  a  further  phased  increase  in  the  allocation  at  the  Revised  Estimates  (RE)  stage
which  would  help  enable  the  organizations  to  scale  up  their  training  programs  and
national-level  jamborees,  ensuring  the  "Scouting  movement"  reaches  every  district  in  the
country.
3.6.3 The  Committee  notes  that  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  is  currently
emphasizing  the  MY  Bharat  platform  as  a  single-window  " phygital "  interface  for  all
youth-related  activities.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  to  integrate  all  major
activities,  workshops,  and  certifications  provided  by  Scouting  and  Guiding  organizations
be  registered  and  tracked  through  the  MY  Bharat  Portal.  Integration  would  provide  these
organizations  with  access  to  a  larger  pool  of  volunteers  and  allow  scouts/guides  to  earn
digital credentials that are part of a unified "Smart CV."
3.6.4 The  Committee  is  of  the  view  that  a  periodic  third-party  impact  assessment  of  the
scouting  and  guiding  programs  funded  by  Government  should  be  institutionalised  and
future  grants  are  made  performance-linked,  based  on  verifiable  data  such  as  the  number  of
" Rashtrapati  Awardees"  produced,  the  success  of  community  service  projects,  and  the
timely submission of Utilization Certificates (UCs) etc .

18
3.7 National  Young  Leaders  Programme  (NYLP):  NYLP  is  a  Scheme  introduced  during
2014-15.  The  Objective  of  the  Scheme  is  to  develop  leadership  qualities  among  the  youth  to  enable
them  to  realize  their  full  potential  and  in  the  process,  to  contribute  to  the  nation-building  process.
The scheme has 3 components, as outlined below:
(i) Neigh bourhood  Youth Parliament (NYP)
(ii)Youth for Development Programme (YFD)
(iii)National Youth Advisory Council (NYAC)
(Note:  Earlier  National  Young  Leaders  Award  (NYLA)  and  National  Youth  Development
Fund  (NYDF)  were  also  the  part  of  the  scheme  but  both  the  components  were  discontinued  as  per
the instructions of Ministry of Finance and Ministry of Home Affairs.)
3.7.1 In  BE  2025-26  Rs  9.25  crores  was  allocated  but  in  RE  2025-26  the  amount  was  increased  to
Rs  21.00  crores.  Both  the  projections  to  Ministry  of  Finance  for  BE  2026-27  and  allocations  stands
at  Rs  10.00  crores.  The  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  from  BE  2026-27  over  RE  2025-26
stands  at  -52.38%.  While  variations  of  percentage  in  allocations  in  BE  2026-27  over  BE  2025-26
stands  at  8.11%.  The  variation  in  BE  2025-26  and  RE  2025-26  due  to  the  fact  that  a dditional
fund  provided  in  RE  2025-26  for  Youth  Parliament  in  all  the  Districts,  States  and  then  at  the  Centre.
The  Department  has  further  informed  that  more  funds  will  be  projected  at  RE  stage  of  Financial  Year
2026-27.
3.7.2 The  Committee  notes  that  the  Neighborhood  Youth  Parliament  (NYP)  is  a  core
component  of  NYLP,  aimed  at  developing  leadership  and  debating  skills  at  the  grassroots
level.  For  BE  2026-27,  the  allocation  for  NYLP  stands  at  ₹ 10.00  crores,  which  is
significantly  lower  than  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  internal  projection  of
approximately  ₹ 17.30  crores  required  for  full-scale  implementation.  The  Committee
recommends  the  Department  to  move  for  an  upward  revision  of  the  NYLP  budget  at  the
Revised  Estimates  (RE)  stage.  A  dedicated  fund  of  ₹ 10  crores  is  insufficient  to  cover  786
districts  effectively  if  the  goal  is  to  make  youth  parliaments  a  monthly  or  quarterly  feature
rather than a one-time annual event.
3.7.3 The  Committee  notes  that  MY  Bharat  platform  is  designed  to  provide  "digital
credentials"  to  youth  for  their  social  contributions.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends
the  Department  to  automatically  reflect  activities/achievements  of  volunteer  in  the
volunteer ’s  Smart  CV  on  the  MY  Bharat  portal  such  as  participation  in  NYLP  activities,
winning  a  district  or  state-level  Youth  Parliament  etc.  This  would  allow  young  leaders  to
showcase  their  leadership  and  public  speaking  credentials  to  potential  employers  or
academic institutions, thereby increasing the aspirational  value of the program.
3.7.4 The  Committee  feels  that  Neighborhood  Youth  Parliaments  serve  as  platforms  for
debate  and  discussion  on  socio-economic  issues.  However,  the  feedback  loop  from  these
discussions  to  the  actual  policy-making  process  remains  informal.  The  Committee,
therefore,  further  recommends  the  Department  to  establish  a  formal  mechanism  where  the
resolutions  or  "Youth  Declarations"  passed  at  State  and  National  Youth  Parliaments  are
compiled  and  shared  with  relevant  Ministries  (such  as  Education,  Health,  or  Rural

19
Development)  which  would  transform  NYLP  from  a  purely  "personality  development"
exercise into a meaningful tool for youth-led governance .
3.9 YUVA  PORTAL  (MY  BHARAT  PORTAL)  AND  MERA  YUVA  BHARAT  (MY
BHARAT):
MY Bharat [erstwhile Nehru Yuva Kendra Sangathan (NYKS)]
3.9.1 Nehru  Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan  (NYKS),  launched  in  1972,  is  one  of  the  largest  youth
organizations  in  the  world.   NYKS  has  presence  in  623  districts  of  the  country  through  Nehru  Yuva
Kendras  (NYKs).   Since  2014,  there  has  been  a  marked  shift  in  the  functioning  of  the  organization
as  it  reoriented  its  programmes  towards  the  greater  goal  of  Youth-led  Development  as  proposed  by
the  Hon ’ble  Prime  Minister.On  31st  October  2023,  the  Hon ’ble  Prime  Minister  launched  the  Mera
Yuva  Bharat  –  MY  Bharat,  a  phygital  platform  (Physical  +  Digital)  for  engaging  youth  in  the  age
bracket  between  15-29  years  in  Nation  Building  activities.  Consequent  to  the  creation  of  MY
Bharat  as  an  autonomous  body  under  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs,  the  resources  of  Nehru
Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan  is  being  utilized  in  MY  Bharat  as  approved  by  the  Cabinet.   The  Board  of
Governors  of  Nehru  Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan,  in  its  meeting  held  on  16.01.2026,  has  approved  the
transfer of the assets and liabilities of Nehru Yuva Kendra Sangathan to MY Bharat.
3.9.2 The  programs  of  MY  Bharat  is  unique  in  its  design  and  implementation  as  it  operates
among  the  youth  population  of  the  country  with  the  spirit  of  volunteerism  self-help  and
participation.   MY  Bharat  shares  an  equal  relationship  with  its  affiliated  Youth  Clubs,  one  that  is
based  on  trust,  cooperation  and  the  common  goal  of  “ Yuva  Bhagidaari  se  Jan  Bhagidari ”  to  ensure
the  development  of  nation.  Another  unique  aspect  of  MY  Bharat  programs  is  that  unlike  other
voluntary  activities,  MY  Bharat  programs  are  implemented  across  all  the  districts  of  the  country  at
a  massive  scale.  Through  these  programs,  MY  Bharat  also  groom  young  leaders  and  empower  them
to  lead  efforts  towards  social  development  in  the  both  rural  and  urban  communities  across  the
country.
3.9.3 The  areas  of  focus  of  the  MY  Bharat  activities  include  education,  health  and  sanitation,
environment,  awareness  on  social  issues,  women  empowerment,  civic  education,  disaster  relief  and
rehabilitation,  etc.  The  youth  associated  with  Nehru  Yuva  Kendras  are  not  only  socially  aware  and
motivated but are also inclined towards social development work through voluntary efforts.
3.9.4 The  principal  objective  of  MY  Bharat  is  to  mobilize,  motivate,  organize  rural  youth  of  the
country  and  enhance  their  capacities  to  develop  democratic  institutional  mechanisms  in  the  form  of
village  based  Youth  Clubs,  empower  them  to  become  productive  and  responsible  citizens,  assume
local  leadership  to  act  as  active  partners  in  the  process  of  community  development  and  nation
building  activities  with  the  spirit  of  equity  and  voluntarism.In  order  to  make  youth  empowered  and
participate  in  the  national  development,  MY  Bharat  organizes  mainly  four  ypes  of  activities  viz.
Core  Programmes,  Special  Programmes,  Schemes  of  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  viz.  National
Program  for  Youth  and  Adolescent  Development  and  National  Young  Leaders  Program  and
Projects  in  collaboration  and  funding  from  other  Ministries  for  Youth  Development  and
Empowerment.
3.9.5 The  Department  stated  that  under  the  Scheme  of  Yuva  Portal , the  budget  outlay  in  BE
and  RE  2025-26  were  Rs. 90.00  Crores  and  47.16  Crores,  respectively,  while  that  of  BE  2026-27
is  Rs.  90.00  Crores.   The  reasons  provided  for  seeking  same  budget  at  BE  2026-27  compared  to

20
BE  2025-26  are  the  revamping  of  MY  Bharat  Portal,  remuneration  for  the  IT  cell,  &  PMU  for
monitoring of MY Bharat Portal. 
3.9.6 The  details  of  projections  of  funds  made  for  Mera  Yuva  Bharat  under  the  Department  of
Youth  Affairs  vis-à-vis  amount  allocated  in  BE  (2026-27)  and  percentage  of  change/variations  over
BE/RE (2025-26) are as follows:

3.9.7 The  Budget  Estimates  (BE),  Revised  Estimates  (RE)  and  Actual  Utilization  (Actuals )
under  the  scheme  states  that  the  BE  and  RE  for  the  FY  2025-26  were  Rs.  82.48  Crores  and  Rs.
66.78  Crores,  respectively.   Projections  to  MoF  for  BE  2026-27  was  Rs.  775.00  Crores  while  the
BE  2026-27  is  Rs.  655.22  Crores . The  percentage  of  change/variations  of  BE  2026-27  over  RE
2025-26  is  881.16%;  BE  2026-27  over  Projections  to  MoF  is  -15.46%  while  the  BE  2026-27
over  BE  2025-26  is  694.40%.  The  Committee  was  informed  that  the  Department  sought  higher
allocation for addition of new units in My Bharat-NSS.
3.9.8 The  Department  has  informed  that  reasons  for  such  an  higher  allocation  is  due  to  creation  of
MY  Bharat  as  an  autonomous  body  under  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs,  the  resources  of  Nehru
Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan  is  being  utilized  in  MY  Bharat  as  approved  by  the  Cabinet.   The  Board  of
Governors  of  Nehru  Yuva  Kendra  Sangathan,  in  its  meeting  held  on  16.01.2026,  has  also  approved
the transfer of the assets and liabilities of Nehru Yuva Kendra Sangathan to MY Bharat.
3.9.9 The  committee  was  informed  that  MY  Bharat  (erstwhile  NYKS)  was  allotted  Rs.
423.00  crores  at  RE  2025-26  and  Actual  expenditure  was  Rs.  318.50  crores  as  on
19.01.2026.  Further,  it  was  also  informed  that  shortage  in  manpower  resulted  in  this
underlisation  of  funds.  While  the  BE  for  2026-27  has  been  significantly  increased  to
₹ 655.22  crores  to  support  the  full  absorption  of  NYKS  staff  and  assets,  persistent  low
utilization  weakens  the  case  for  further  funding . The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  to
implement  a  robust  institutional  monitoring  framework  to  ensure  optimal  and  timely
utilization  of  the  increased  ₹ 655.22  crores  allocation . Besides,  the  Department  should  make
sincere  e fforts  to  streamline  administrative  processes  for  asset  transfer  from  NYKS  to  MY
Bharat  and  ensuring  that  quarterly  expenditure  plans  are  strictly  followed  to  avoid  end-of-
year "parking" of funds .

3.9.12 The  Committee  notes  that  there  is  a  massive  shortage  of  manpower  in  MY  Bharat,
autonomous  body.  As  of  December  31,  2025,  MY  Bharat  has  1,413  vacancies  against  a
sanctioned  strength  of  2,000  posts . Specifically,  key  leadership  and  field  positions  remain
unoccupied,  including  4  Director  posts , 22  Joint/State  Director  posts,  and  44  Deputy
Director  posts . The  Committee  recommends  the  Department  to  launch  a  time-bound
special  recruitment  drive  to  fill  these  1,413  vacancies  and  ensure  that  the  nationwide
expansion  and  operational  effectiveness  of  the  MY  Bharat  platform  are  not  compromised
due to a lack of human resources .
3.9.13 The  Department  has  informed  that  MY  Bharat  recognized  the  detrimental  impact  of  drug
addiction  and  substance  abuse  on  individuals  and  families  and  is  conducting  Awareness  and
Education  Programme  on  Drug-addiction  and  Substance  Abuse.  The  aim  of  the  programme  is  to
increase  awareness  by  educating  youth  about  the  harmful  effects  of  drug  use,  including  physical,
psychological  and  social  consequences,  promoting  healthy  alternatives  by  encouraging
engagement  in  positive  activities  like  sports,  arts,  cultural  events  and  community  service.  The

3.9.14 The  Department  Youth  Affairs  also  apprised  the  Committee  about   observance
of  International  Yoga  Day  on  21st  June  with  a  view  to  generate  awareness  on  Yoga  and  motivate
youth  and  others  to  practice  Yoga  as  part  of  their  daily  routine  activities  emphasising  its  benefits
for physical and mental health by MY Bharat.
3.9.15 The  Committee  notes  that  MY  Bharat  has  successfully  crossed  2.07  crores  youth
registrations,  its  primary  mandate  is  to  act  as  a  " phygital "  platform  that  bridges  the  gap
between  digital  opportunities  and  grassroots  engagement.  It  was  also  informed  that  MY

25
Bharat  (subsuming  NYKS)  is  the  principal  vehicle  for  mobilizing  rural  youth  through
2.58 lakh  village-based  Youth  Clubs.  The  Committee  recommends  to  ensure  that  youth  in
remote  and  marginalized  areas  are  not  excluded  from  the  digital  ecosystem  and  to  prevent
a  digital  divide,  "offline"  outreach  mechanisms  should  be  strengthen  and  should  include
increasing  the  number  of  Future  Youth  Leaders  Bootcamps  and  workshops  on  flagship
schemes specifically in under-represented districts.
3.9.16 The  Committee  is  of  the  view  that  MY  Bharat  connects  youth  clubs  throughout  the
country.  These  clubs,  therefore,  should  be  empowered  with  "Campaign  kits"  (digital  and
physical)  to  host  street  plays,  Marathons,  or  Nukad  Nataks  specifically  during  local
festivals  when  substance  use  may  hike.  Use  of  geo-location  data  to  identify  directly  with
high  youths  registration  but  low  "drug  free"  activity  engagement  would  also  help  identify
high rist  aones  and better use of resources.
3.9.17 The  Committee  notes  that  MY  Bharat  serves  as  the  executing  agency  for  the
National  Youth  Corps  (NYC)  scheme,  where  volunteers  receive  an  honorarium  of  ₹ 5,000
per  month . The  Committee  notes  that  the  volunteer  base  is  under  expansion  and  a  proposal
for  enhanced  honorarium  is  currently  under  consideration  by  the  Department . The
Committee,  therefore,  recommends  that  considering  current  economic  standards  and  the
critical  role  these  volunteers  play  in  grassroots  nation-building  and  disaster  response,  the
proposal  to  increase  the  monthly  honorarium  beyond  the  existing  ₹ 5,000  to  attract  higher-
quality  talent  should  be  extended  as  well  as  to  ensure  the  long-term  sustainability  of  the
volunteer network .

26
3.10 MY Bharat- NATIONAL SERVICE SCHEME (NSS)
3.10.1 MY  Bharat-  National  Service  Scheme  (NSS)  was  introduced  with  the  primary  objective
of  developing  the  personality  and  character  of  student  youth  through  voluntary  community
service.  ‘Education  through  Service ’ is  the  purpose  of  the  NSS.  The  ideological  orientation  of  the
NSS  is  inspired  by  the  ideals  of  Mahatma  Gandhi.  Very  appropriately,  the  motto  of  NSS  is  “ NOT
ME, BUT YOU ” . An NSS volunteer places the ‘community ’ before ‘self ’. 
3.10.2 NSS aims at developing the following qualities/ competencies among the volunteers:
(i) To  understand  the  community  in  which  the  NSS  volunteers  work  and  to  understand
themselves in relation to their community;
(ii) To  identify  the  needs  and  problems  of  the  community  and  involve  themselves  in  problem-
solving exercise;
(iii) To develop among themselves a sense of social and civic responsibility;
(iv) To  utilize  their  knowledge  in  finding  practical  solutions  to  individual  and  community
problems;
(v) To gain skills in mobilizing community participation;
(vi) To acquire leadership qualities and democratic values;
(vii) To develop capacity to meet emergencies and natural disasters; and
(viii) To practice national integration and social harmony.
3.10.3 NSS  attempts  to  establish  meaningful  linkages  between  ‘Campus  and  Community ’, ‘College
and  Village ’ and  ‘Knowledge  and  Action ’. The  Department  has  informed  about  Physical  Targets  &
Achievements  for  the  last  3  years  under  MY  Bharat-National  Service  Scheme  (NSS)  given  at
TABLE-17

3.10.5 The  details  of  Budget  Estimates  (BE),  Revised  Estimates  (RE)  and  Actual  Utilization
(Actuals ) states  that   the  BE  and  RE  for  the  FY  2025-26  were  Rs.  450.00  Crores  and  Rs.  275.00
Crores,  respectively.   Projections  to  MoF  for  BE  2026-27  was  Rs.  550.50  Crores  while  the  BE
2026-27  is  Rs.  357.39  Crores . The  percentage  of  change/variations  of  BE  2026-27  over  RE
2025-26  is  29.96%;  BE  2026-27  over  Projections  to  MoF  is  -35.02%  while  the  BE  2026-27  over
BE  2025-26  is  -20.58%.  The  Committee  was  informed  that  the  Department  sought  higher
allocation for addition of new units in My Bharat-NSS.
3.10.6 Further,  The  Department  has  informed  the  Committee  that  as  on  31st  December,2025  the
total  sanctioned  strength  of  administrative  staff  and  other  categories  including  Group  ‘A ’
(Gazetted ),  Group  ‘B ’ (Gazetted  and  Non- Gazetted ) and  Group  ‘C ’in  the  National  Service
Scheme  is  199  posts.  Out  of  these  sanctioned  posts,  120  posts  are  currently  vacant,  indicating  an
overall vacancy position of approximately 60.30%.
3.10.7 The  Committee  notes  the  vacancy  positions  within  the  administrative  and
operational  framework  of  the  National  Service  Scheme  (NSS).  As  of  31st  December  2025,
the  Department  reported  that  out  of  a  total  sanctioned  strength  of  199  posts  across  various
categories — including  Group  ‘A ’ (Gazetted ),  Group  ‘B ’ (Gazetted  and  Non- Gazetted ),  and
Group  ‘C ’— a  staggering  120  posts  remain  vacant  constitute  an  overall  vacancy  rate  of

29
approximately  60.30  per  cent,  a  situation  where  it  is  difficult  to  manage  for  a  premier
national  youth  organization.  Further,  to  support  the  proposed  addition  of  25,000  new  NSS
units  in  the  2026-27  cycle , the  Committee  recommends  to  ensure  that  Regional  Directorates
are  staffed  at  100  per  cent  capacity.  Since,  administrative  staff  at  the  regional  level  are  the
primary  link  between  the  Ministry  and  educational  institutions,  the  Committee
recommends  to  initiate  an  emergency,  time-bound  recruitment  process  to  fill  all  120  vacant
positions  latest  by  31 st  December,  2026  as  without  this  the  objective  of  education  through
service  cannot  be  monitored  effectively.  The  current  reliance  on  a  mere  39.70  per  cent  of
the  sanctioned  workforce  is  unsustainable  and  severely  compromises  the  Department's
ability to provide effective oversight and guidance to its millions of volunteers.
3.10.8  The  Committee  notes  that  various  states  have  taken  initiative  to  reward  and
recognize  the  contributions  of  NSS  volunteers,  but  that  there  is  a  wide  variation  in  the
policy  adopted  by  different  states.  The  Committee  recommends  to  bridge  this  gap  by
developing  and  radiating  a  common  framework  for  the  same.  In  this  regard,  the
Committee  recommends  that  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  should  work  with  the
Department  of  Higher  Education  to  ensure  that  service  hours  undertaken  by  NSS
volunteers are creditized  in the Academic Bank of Credits accepted by HEIs.
 3.11 RAJIV GANDHI NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF YOUTH DEVELOPMENT
3.11.1 RGNIYD,  Sriperumbudur , Tamil  Nadu  is  an  Institute  of  National  Importance  established
by  the  Act  of  Parliament  No.35/12  and  a  premier  organisation  of  the  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs
and  Sports,  Government  of  India.   RGNIYD  offers  academic  programmes  at  post  graduate  and
doctoral  levels,  engaging  in  seminal  research  in  the  vital  areas  of  youth  development  besides
functioning  as  functions  as  a  vital  resource  centre  with  its  multi-faceted  functions  of  conducting
training  programmes  for  youth  functionaries,  spearheading  extension  and  outreach  initiatives
across the country.

3.11.4 The  Committee  was  apprised  the  Committee  that  during  the  year  under  report,  RGNIYD

work  and  were  recommended  for  award  of  Ph.D  degrees.  RGNIYD  continued  to  offer  academic

in  Animation;  PGD  in  Game  Design;  and  PGD  in  Visual  Communication  Design.  RGNIYD
presently  offers  various  skill-based  academic  programs  under  MoA  with  Creative    Arts
Education  Society  (CAES),  New  Delhi  which  include  B.  Des  in  Communication  Design;  B.  Des
in  Fashion  Communication;  B.  Des  in  Fashion  Design;  B.  Des  in  Fashion  Styling;  B.Des  in
Interior  Architectural  Design;  B.  Des  in  Product  Design;  B.  Des  in  Textile  Design;  BBA;  BBA  in
Fashion  and  Lifestyle  Business  Management;  BBA  in  Global  Luxury  Brand  Management;  M.
Des  in  Communication  Design;  M.  Des  in  Fashion  Communication;  M.  Des  in  Fashion  Design;
M.  Des  in  Fashion  Styling;  M.  Des  in  Interior  Design;  M.  Des  in  Product  Design;  MBA  in
Fashion  and  Lifestyle  Business  Management;  MBA  in  Global  Luxury  Brand  Management;  and
MBA. 
3.11.5 The  Committee  was  also,  informed  by  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  that  through   training
and   orientation   activities,   RGNIYD   organized   training   of  trainers  programmes  on  themes
such  as  Youth  Rights  and  Legal  Aid;  Promotion  of  Youth  Mental  Health;  Life  Skills  Education
apart  from  offering  various  capacity  building  programmes  for  youth  on  topics  such  as  Legal
Awareness  and  Leadership  Development;  Leadership  and  Community;  Youth  Employability
Skills;  Life  Skills  Education;  Leadership  and  Community  Development,  orientation  and  capacity
building  programmes  of  youth  work  professionals  and  youth.  RGNIYD  organized  several
batches  of  master  trainers  programmes  and  capacity-building  programmes  across  Tamil  Nadu,
Kerala,  Gujarat,  Daman  &  Diu,  etc.,  trained  hundreds  of  NSS  Programme  Officers  and  faculty
for MY Bharat. 

31
3.11.6 With  a  view  to  empowering  the  marginalized  Youth  in  Kashmir,  RGNIYD  in
collaboration    with  the  Snow  Leopard  Brigade,  Indian  Army  in  Gurez  Valley  of  Kashmir,
embarked  on  a  project  to  offer  skill  training  in  local  specific  trades  such  as  Crewel  Craft  and
Sozni  and  Shawl   Making,   besides,   training   the   youth   in   Tally   Software   for   enhancing   their
employability  skills  for  open  employment.  9  batches  of  training  were  conducted  each  of  duration
of  3  months  impacting  the  lives  of  over  200  youth  in  remote  areas  of  Gurez  Valley,  Kashmir
thereby  promoting  self-reliance  and  entrepreneurial  skills  in  conflict-affected  zones  through
handicraft  and  financial  literacy  trainings.  Pioneering  in  the  domain  of  Mental  Health  and  Life
Skills  Education,  RGNIYD  and  its  Regional  Centre  at  Chandigarh  consistently  reached  NSS
volunteers  and  students  by  building  emotional  resilience  and  promoting  Youth  Mental  Health
which marked a shift toward prioritizing youth well-being.
 3.11.7 The  Institute  championed  initiatives  of  Community  Engagement  and  Social
Responsibility  in  Kachipattu    village,    Sriperumbudur   by    offering   Entrepreneurship
Development   and  Community  outreach  services  through  the  Nook  Learning  Centre
collaboration which marked RGNIYD's grounded approach to rural and tribal empowerment.
3.11.8 The  Committee  recognises  the  pivotal  role  of  the  Rajiv  Gandhi  National  Institute  of
Youth  Development  (RGNIYD)  as  an  Institute  of  National  Importance  and  the  premier
"think-tank"  for  youth-related  activities  in  the  country.  While  the  Budget  Estimate  (BE)
for  2026-27  is  set  at  ₹ 26.50  crores — a  marginal  increase  from  the  previous  year — the
Committee  believes  this  allocation  may  be  insufficient  to  meet  the  Institute ’s  expanding
national  mandate.  The  Committee  is  of  the  view  that  as  an  Institute  of  National
Importance,  RGNIYD  should  not  be  constrained  by  a  "marginal  increase"  in  funding
under  BE  for  FY  2026-27.  The  Department  should  present  a  detailed  requirement  for
additional  grants  at  the  RE  stage  to  ensure  RGNIYD  is  financially  sound  to  fully  realize  its
potential as a global leader in youth development

3.11.10 The  Committee  notes  that  there  is  a  "shortage  of  manpower"  as  a  primary
challenge  in  implementing  youth-related  programs . As  of  31 st  December,  2025,  the
sanctioned  strength  for  the  RGNIYD  is  65 , h owever  the  actual  number  of  employees  in
position  remains  significantly  lower  at  43  which  indicates  a  persistent  vacancy  rate  of

approximately  33.84  per  cent.  Further,  there  is  100  per  cent  vacancy  for  the  post  of
Director,  Registrar,  Professors,  Controller  of  Examination  and  Finance  Officer  even  after
the  Committee  highlighted  the  same  in  its  Report  No  366.  RGNIYD  is  notified  as  an
'Institute  of  National  Importance,'  the  lack  of  filled  academic  and  administrative  positions
hinders  its  role  as  a  "think-tank"  and  a  mentor  for  the  nationwide  networks  of  MY  Bharat
and  NSS . The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  to  fill-up  all  the  existing  vacancies  in  a
time-bound  manner  and  not  later  that  31 st  December,  2026  and  apprise  the  Committee  of
the same.

4 . OVERVIEW OF THE DEPARTMENT OF SPORTS
4 . 1 The  Ministry  of  Youth  Affairs  &  Sports  was  initially  set  up  as  the  Department
of  Sports  in  1982  at  the  time  of  the  organization  of  the  IX  Asian  Games  in  New  Delhi. 
Its  name  was  changed  to  the  Department  of  Youth  Affairs  &  Sports  during  celebration
of  the  International  Youth  Year,  1985.   It  became  a  Ministry  on  27 th  May,  2000. 
Subsequently,  the  Ministry  has  been  bifurcated  in  Department.  of  Youth  Affairs  and
Department  of  Sports  under  two  separate  Secretaries  w.e.f .  30 th  April,2008.    The
specific  subjects  being  dealt  with  by  these  two  Departments  are  contained  in  the  Order
of the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961.
4 . 2 Sports  promotion  is  primarily  the  responsibility  of  the  various  National  Sports
Federations  which  are  autonomous.   The  role  of  the  Government  is  to  create  the
infrastructure  and  promote  capacity  building  for  broad-basing  sports  as  well  as  for
achieving  excellence  in  various  competitive  events  at  the  national  and  international
levels.  The Department schemes are geared towards achieving these objectives.
V ision and Mission
4 .3  Sports  and  games  are  crucial  for  holistic  human  development,  serving  not  just  as
entertainment  and  physical  exercise,  but  also  fostering  a  spirit  of  healthy  competition
and  community  bonding.  The  significance  of  sports  for  the  nation  has  transcended
from  merely  showcasing  excellence  on  the  global  stage  to  playing  a  multifaceted  role
in  contributing  to  the  holistic  growth  of  the  country  by  promoting  healthy  lifestyles,
driving  economic  activities,  fostering  community  engagement,  and  providing
opportunities  for  national  pride  through  international  achievements.  Recognizing  this
multidimensional  importance  in  nation-building,  the  Government  of  India  has
implemented  several  key  schemes  and  initiatives  through  the  Department  of  Sports,
aimed  at  supporting  athletes  and  stakeholders  in  reaching  their  full  potential  across
disciplines.  With  flagship  programs  like  Khelo  India,  the  National  Sports  University,
and  the  National  Centre  for  Sports  Science  and  Research,  the  aim  is  to  develop  India's
sporting infrastructure and capabilities at par with major nations. 
4 .4 While  sports  garnered  attention  for  decades,  it  received  dedicated  push  after
hosting  the  1982  Asian  Games,  leading  to  the  National  Sports  Policies  of  1984,  2001
and  2025.Khelo  Bharat  NITI  2025  focuses  on  achieving  the  nation ’ s  Excellence  on  the
Global  Stage  by  enhancing  infrastructure,  talent  identification  and  international
competitiveness.  Additionally,  Sports  for  Economic  Development  emphasizes
leveraging  sports  to  boost  industries  such  as  tourism,  manufacturing  and  sports
technology.  Further,  Sports  for  Social  Development  promotes  inclusivity,  health  and
education  through  sports,  fostering  societal  cohesion.  KBN  also  focuses  on  Sports  –  A
People ’ s  Movement  seeks  to  engage  communities  in  physical  activities,  making  sports
a  cultural  cornerstone.  Finally,  the  Khelo  Bharat  Niti-2025  aligns  with  the  National
Education  Policy  (NEP)  2020,  integrating  sports  with  education  to  nurture  holistic

35
development  and  life  skills  in  students,  youth,  promoting  both  academic  and  athletic
excellence.
4 .5 As  modern  sports  have  become  highly  competitive,  the  use  of  advanced
infrastructure,  equipment,  and  scientific  support  has  transformed  the  international
sporting  landscape.  Recognizing  the  growing  demands  for  such  state-of-the-art
resources,  the  Government  of  India  has  also  undertaken  several  initiatives  to  assist
athletes  by  providing  training,  exposure  to  international  competitions,  and  access  to
scientific  backing  and  cutting-edge  equipment.  This  comprehensive  support  aims  to
equip Indian sportspersons with the tools necessary to excel on the world stage.
4 . 6  DEPARTMENT OF SPORTS –  ORGANOGRAM :
(a) Sports Authority of India (SAI)
(i) 12 Regional Centres (RCs)

Affairs and Sports has been presented to Parliament under the following Demand Numbers:
Demand No. 102- Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports
This  Report  pertains  to  the  Demands  for  Grants  (Demand  No.  102)  relating  to  Department
of Sports.

Cr  out  of  which  ₹  3113.36  Cr  has  been  allocated  to  Department  of  Sports,  which  is
69.50% of the total budget of the Ministry.

5 . 4  The  budgetary  trends  of  the  Department  of  Sports  indicate  a  structured  expansion
in  financial  allocation  over  the  assessment  period,  reflecting  continued  policy
emphasis  on  sports  development,  infrastructure  augmentation,  and  programmatic
strengthening.
5.5 During  2024 – 25,  the  Department  recorded  Actual  Expenditure  of  ₹ 2,136.99
crore  under  Revenue  and  ₹ 2,520.53  crore  under  Capital.  For  2025 – 26,  the  Budget
Estimates  (BE)  placed  the  Revenue  allocation  at  ₹ 2,347.14  crore.  However,  the
Revised  Estimates  (RE)  for  2025 – 26  show  a  substantial  upward  revision  to  ₹ 3,755.26
crore  under  Revenue,  suggesting  enhanced  implementation  requirements,  acceleration
of  ongoing  schemes,  scaling  up  of  flagship  initiatives,  or  additional  commitments

38
undertaken  during  the  financial  year.  The  absence  of  fresh  capital  allocations  in
2025 – 26  indicates  a  possible  consolidation  phase  in  capital  spending,  with  greater
emphasis on revenue-driven program execution.
5.6 For  2026 – 27,  the  Department  projected  a  Revenue  requirement  of  ₹ 3,111.91
crore  to  the  Ministry  of  Finance.  The  approved  Budget  Estimate  (BE)  for  2026 – 27
stands  at  ₹ 3,113.36  crore,  which  is  marginally  higher  than  the  projected  amount,
indicating near-alignment between departmental requirements and final approval.
5 . 7 The  Committee  notes  the  sharp  upward  revision  in  Revenue  expenditure
from  ₹ 2,347.14  crore  at  the  Budget  Estimates  stage  to  ₹ 3,755.26  crore  at  the
Revised  Estimates  stage  during  2025 – 26.  While  such  enhancement  may  reflect
accelerated  implementation  of  schemes  and  additional  commitments,  the
Committee  is  of  the  view  that  the  Department  should  strengthen  its  budget
expenditure  planning  mechanisms  to  minimize  substantial  mid-year  revisions  and
proper utilization of the Budgetary allocation for the FY 2026-27 .
5.8 The  Committee  observes  that  the  absence  of  fresh  Capital  allocation  in  FY
2025 – 26,  indicating  a  possible  shift  toward  consolidation  of  existing
infrastructure  and  emphasis  on  revenue-driven  execution.  While  prioritizing
program  delivery  is  commendable,  the  Committee  recommends  that  the
Department  ensure  that  critical  capital  investments  are  not  deferred  in  a  manner
that  may  adversely  affect  long-term  capacity  building  and  asset  creation.  A
balanced  approach  between  Revenue  and  Capital  expenditure  should  be
maintained to ensure sustainable growth and institutional strengthening.
5.9 With  regard  to  FY  2026 – 27,  the  Committee  appreciates  the  near-alignment
between  the  Department ’ s  projected  Revenue  requirement  of  ₹ 3,111.91  crore  and
the  approved  Budget  Estimate  of  ₹ 3,113.36  crore,  which  reflects  improved
coordination  with  the  Ministry  of  Finance.  The  Committee  recommends  that  this
realistic  and  evidence-based  budgeting  practice  be  institutionalized  through
strengthened  outcome  monitoring,  periodic  expenditure  reviews,  and  bette r  inter-
divisional  coordination,  which  would  ensure  optimal  utilization  of  allocated
funds .
5.10 A  comparative  analysis  of  Budget  Estimates  shows  that  BE  2026 – 27  reflects
a  23.52  per  cent  increase  over  BE  2025 – 26 ,  underscoring  a  continued  upward
trajectory  in  planned  expenditure  and  reaffirming  the  commitment  of  the
Government  to  strengthening  the  sports  ecosystem.  However,  when  compared
with  the  projections  submitted  for  2026 – 27,  the  approved  BE  represents  a  17.09
per  cent  moderation ,  indicating  prudent  fiscal  rationalization  at  the  stage  of
budget  finalization.  Overall,  the  financial  pattern  demonstrates  a  sustained
expansion  in  allocations  for  the  sports  sector,  coupled  with  careful  fiscal
calibration.  T he  Committee  appreciates  t he  trend  as  a  balanced
approach — ensuring enhanced funding support for development.

39

5 .1 1  The  Committee  notes  significant  variations  between  Budget  Estimates  (BE)  and
Revised  Estimates  (RE)  in  several  schemes  such  as  National  Centre  for  Sports  Science  and
Research , where  the  RE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 78.64  crore)  far  exceeds  BE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 10.00  crore),  and
Khelo  India , where  RE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 700  crore)  is  substantially  lower  than  BE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 1000
crore).  Such  deviations  indicate  weaknesses  in  expenditure  planning  and  implementation
capacity  has  not  been  either  planned  or  monitored  properly . T he  Committee  is  of  the  view
that  the  Department  should  institute  a  robust  quarterly  expenditure  monitoring  mechanism
and strengthen outcome-based budgeting to minimize sharp fluctuations between BE and RE.
5 .1 2 The  allocation  under  Special  Cash  Awards  has  increased  moderately  i.e.  ₹ 36  crore  in
BE  2026 – 27,  while  Pension  to  Meritorious  Sportspersons  shows  a  reduction  from  ₹ 10  crore  at
RE  2025 – 26  to  ₹ 4  crore  at  BE  2026 – 27 . Given  India ’s  expanding  international  sporting
footprint,  sustained  financial  security  for  athletes  is  essential.The  Committee  recommends
creating  a  consolidated  Athlete  Welfare  Corpus  integrating  pension,  medical  assistance,  and
emergency  support  under  the  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National  Welfare  for  Sportspersons

44
scheme  to  ensure  long-term  financial  stability  for  retired  and  indigent  sportspersons.  Pension
allocations  should  reflect  realistic  beneficiary  assessments  rather  than  reverting  to  lower  BE
levels.
5 .1 3 The  flagship  Khelo  India  programme  shows  a  decline  from  BE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 1000  crore)
to  BE  2026 – 27  ( ₹ 924.35  crore),  along  with  underutilisation  in  RE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 700  crore).  This
may  affect  grassroots  sports  development  and  talent  identification.  The  Department  should
conduct  a  third-party  impact  assessment  of  Khelo  India  verticals,  particularly  infrastructure
creation  and  athlete  scholarships.  Funds  saved  due  to  implementation  bottlenecks  should  be
reallocated  within  the  scheme  to  athlete  development  and  coaching  support  to  maintain
continuity in talent pipelines.
5 .1 4 The  Committee  observes  sharp  fluctuations  in  allocation  for  National  Centre  for  Sports
Science  and  Research , while  allocations  for  National  Anti-Doping  Agency  ( ₹ 20.30  crore  in  BE
2026 – 27)  have  been  reduced  despite  rising  global  anti-doping  compliance  requirements.
Simultaneously,  contribution  to  World  Anti-Doping  Agency  has  increased.  India ’s  aspiration
to  become  a  global  sporting  power  requires  sustained  investment  in  sports  science  and  anti-
doping  infrastructure.  The  Committee  recommends  a  multi-year  funding  roadmap  for  sports
science  hubs  under  a  hub-and-spoke  model  and  restoration  of  adequate  funding  to  NADA  to
ensure compliance with the World Anti-Doping Code and protect athlete credibility.
5 .1 5 A  substantial  allocation  of  ₹ 500  crore  has  been  introduced  under  “ Promotion  of  Sports
Goods  Manufacturing .”  While  this  initiative  has  the  potential  to  boost  domestic
manufacturing  and  exports,  the  absence  of  prior  allocation  suggests  the  need  for  clear
operational  guidelines.  The  Department  should  prepare  a  detailed  scheme  framework
outlining  eligibility  criteria,  technology  upgradation  components,  MSME  integration,  and
export  promotion  strategy.  Convergence  with  the  Ministry  of  MSME  and  DPIIT  should  be
ensured  to  avoid  duplication  and  to  maximize  Make-in-India  benefits  in  sports  equipment
manufacturing.
5 .1 6 The  allocation  for  Sports  Authority  of  India  has  increased  to  ₹ 917.38  crore,  while  the
National  Sports  University  shows  a  significant  reduction  from  RE  2025 – 26  ( ₹ 78.64  crore)  to
BE  2026 – 27  ( ₹ 46.98  crore).  Given  the  importance  of  institutional  capacity  in  producing
world-class  athletes  and  sports  scientists,  inconsistent  funding  may  hinder  long-term
development.  The  Committee  recommends  that  institutional  grants  be  linked  to  clearly
defined  performance  indicators,  including  medal  outcomes,  athlete  retention,  academic
outputs,  and  infrastructure  utilization.  A  medium-term  expenditure  framework  should  be
adopted  to  ensure  predictable  funding  for  key  institutions  like  SAI,  LNIPE,  and  National
Sports University to support sustained excellence.
