5 .1 7  The  Committee  note  that  the  total  allocation  under  the  Demand  for  Grants  for  the
Department  of  Sports  has  been  placed  at  ₹ 3,113.36  crore  in  BE  2026 – 27  as  against  ₹ 2,520.53
crore  in  BE  2025 – 26  and  the  Revised  Estimate  of  ₹ 2,347.14  crore  for  2025 – 26 . This  reflects  an
overall  increase  of  23.52  per  cent  over  BE  2025 – 26  and  32.65  per  cent  over  RE  2025 – 26 . The
Committee  observe  that  the  substantial  increase  in  allocation  indicates  a  renewed  thrust  on
sports  development,  particularly  in  relation  to  infrastructure  expansion,  preparation  for
major  international  sporting  events  such  as  the  Asian  Games  2026  and  Commonwealth
Games  2026 , strengthening  of  the  anti-doping  framework,  and  expansion  of  flagship
programmes.  The  Committee  view  the  enhanced  outlay  as  a  positive  step  towards  improving
India ’s competitiveness in international sports.
5 .18 The  Committee  further  note  that  an  allocation  of  ₹ 31.85  crore  has  been  made  under
Secretariat  –  Department  of  Sports  in  BE  2026 – 27 , whereas  no  provision  existed  in  BE
2025 – 26  owing  to  the  earlier  booking  of  establishment  expenditure  under  the  Department  of
Youth  Affairs.  The  present  allocation  reflects  the  splitting  of  establishment  expenditure
between  the  two  Departments . While  the  Committee  acknowledge  that  this  adjustment
improves  administrative  clarity,  they  observe  that  such  restructuring  affects  year-to-year
comparability  of  expenditure.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommend  that  the  Ministry
provide  detailed  explanatory  notes  in  future  Demands  clearly  outlining  the  implications  of
such changes so as to facilitate accurate analysis of budgetary trends.
5.19 With  regard  to  institutional  development,  the  Committee  observe  that  the  allocation  to
the  Sports  Authority  of  India  has  increased  moderately  to  ₹ 917.38  crore  in  BE  2026 – 27  from
₹ 830.00  crore  in  BE  2025 – 26 . The  enhancement  is  stated  to  be  on  account  of  scheme
expansion,  infrastructure  commitments,  equipment  procurement,  athlete  preparation  and
settlement  of  certain  legacy  liabilities.  The  Committee  further  note  that  provisions  relating  to
anti-doping  mechanisms,  including  support  to  the  National  Dope  Testing  Laboratory  and  the
National  Anti-Doping  Agency , have  been  rationalized  in  line  with  operational  requirements
and  the  provisions  of  the  National  Anti-Doping  Act.  While  recognizing  the  importance  of
regulatory  strengthening  and  scientific  testing,  the  Committee  recommend  that  the  Ministry
establish  measurable  outcome  indicators  to  ensure  that  increased  institutional  funding  leads
to  improved  athlete  performance,  enhanced  training  facilities  and  strengthened  research
capabilities.

48
5 .2 0 The  Committee  also  note  that  the  allocation  to  the  National  Sports  University  has
declined  in  BE  2026 – 27  as  compared  to  both  BE  and  RE  of  the  previous  year,  which  has  been
attributed  to  the  nearing  completion  of  major  capital  works  relating  to  the  permanent  campus
at  Imphal.  While  the  Committee  acknowledge  that  the  reduction  is  in  line  with  the  project
cycle,  they  stress  that  adequate  resources  must  be  ensured  for  the  transition  from
infrastructure  creation  to  full  academic,  research  and  training  operations.  The  Committee
recommend  that  the  Ministry  present  a  comprehensive  operational  roadmap  for  the
University,  including  faculty  recruitment,  development  of  sports  science  programmes  and
collaboration with national training centres .
5 .2 1 The  Committee  further  observe  that  allocations  under  schemes  relating  to
encouragement  and  welfare  of  sportspersons  have  witnessed  mixed  trends.  While  provisions
for  Special  Cash  Awards , Human  Resource  Development  in  Sports , and  assistance  to  National
Sports  Federations  have  been  enhanced,  allocations  under  certain  welfare  schemes  remain
modest.  The  Committee  are  of  the  view  that  athlete-centric  support  measures  are  critical  for
sustaining  motivation  and  ensuring  financial  security  of  sportspersons.  The  Committee
therefore  recommend  that  the  Ministry  periodically  review  the  adequacy  of  incentive  schemes
and  strengthen  direct  support  mechanisms  to  ensure  that  outstanding  sportspersons  receive
timely recognition and financial assistance.
5 .2 2 The  Committee  note  with  particular  interest  the  introduction  of  a  new  scheme  for
Promotion  of  Sports  Goods  Manufacturing  with  a  substantial  allocation  of  ₹ 500  crore  in  BE
2026 – 27 . The  Committee  view  this  initiative  as  an  important  step  towards  strengthening  the
domestic  sports  ecosystem  and  enhancing  India ’s  capacity  in  sports  equipment  production.
The  Committee  recommend  that  the  scheme  be  implemented  in  close  coordination  with
industry  stakeholders  so  as  to  promote  innovation,  quality  standards  and  export  potential  in
the sports goods sector.
5 .2 3 The  Committee  further  observe  that  the  allocation  under  the  Khelo  India  Scheme
remains  substantial  at  ₹ 924.35  crore  in  BE  2026 – 27 , reflecting  the  continued  emphasis  on
grassroots  sports  development,  talent  identification  and  strengthening  of  sports  infrastructure
across  the  country.  The  Committee  also  note  increased  provisions  relating  to  preparations  for
international  competitions  and  associated  infrastructure  commitments.  The  Committee
emphasize  that  effective  monitoring,  timely  completion  of  projects  and  rigorous  evaluation  of
outcomes  are  essential  to  ensure  optimal  utilization  of  public  funds.  The  Committee,
therefore,  recommend  that  the  Ministry  submit  periodic  progress  reports  to  Parliament
detailing both physical and financial achievements under major schemes and programmes.

6 .1  Incentive to Sports Persons
6 .1.1 The  Scheme  of  Incentive  to  Sportspersons  is  aimed  at  recognizing  and  rewarding
outstanding  achievements  by  Indian  athletes  in  international  sporting  events  such  as  the  Olympics,
Paralympics,  Asian  Games,  Commonwealth  Games,  and  World  Championships.  It  serves  as  a
motivational  instrument  to  encourage  excellence  in  sports  and  to  foster  a  culture  of  high
performance.  The  scheme  ensures  that  medal  winners  and  their  coaches  receive  timely  financial
rewards in recognition of their contribution to national prestige.

50
6 .1.2 The  scheme  not  only  provides  cash  incentives  but  also  reinforces  the  Government ’s
commitment  towards  supporting  elite  athletes.  By  institutionalizing  financial  recognition,  it
strengthens  athlete  morale,  promotes  competitive  spirit,  and  enhances  India ’s  global  sporting
stature.  It  also  ensures  transparency  and  uniformity  in  reward  distribution  across  sporting
disciplines.
6 .1.3 From  a  budgetary  perspective,  allocations  under  this  scheme  reflect  performance-linked
expenditure  and  vary  depending  on  the  number  of  medal-winning  achievements  in  major
international  events.  The  funds  are  demand-driven  and  disbursed  promptly  to  ensure  credibility  and
trust  in  the  system.  However,  fluctuations  in  allocations  are  observed  due  to  the  cyclical  nature  of
major sporting events.
6 .1.4 Under  the  Head  “ Incentives  to  Sports  Persons ”  the  BE  of  Rs.  37.00  crore  was  reduced  to
Rs.28.00  crore  at  the  RE  stage  for  the  Financial  Year  2025-26.  The  fund  allocation  in  BE  2026-27
is  Rs.  40.00  crores.  The  scheme  reflects  moderate  but  stable  financial  support  towards  rewarding
international  medal  winners.  The  Actual  Expenditure  in  2024 – 25  stood  at  ₹ 42.38  crore,  while  BE
2025 – 26  was  ₹ 37.00  crore,  revised  downward  to  ₹ 28.00  crore  at  RE  stage.  For  2026 – 27,  the
Ministry  projected  ₹ 46.00  crore,  against  which  ₹ 40.00  crore  has  been  approved  at  BE  stage.  The  BE
2026 – 27  shows  an  increase  of  42.86%  over  RE  2025 – 26  and  8.11%  over  BE  2025 – 26,  indicating
renewed emphasis on performance-linked incentives.
6 .1.5 However,  the  approved  BE  is  13.04%  lower  than  the  projection  made  to  the  Ministry  of
Finance,  suggesting  partial  rationalization.  Given  that  the  scheme  is  demand-driven  and  linked  to
medal  outcomes,  variability  in  allocations  reflects  the  cyclical  nature  of  international  sporting
events.
6 .1.6 The  schemes  under  the  Incentives  to  Sports  Persons  Head  are  demand  driven  schemes  and
the  expenditure  depends  upon  the  number  of  eligible  applications  received  for  cash  incentive,
financial  assistance  and  pension.  Further,  due  to  low  number  of  eligible  applications  in  the  current
Financial  Year  led  to  the  reduction  in  the  allocation  of  funds.  However,  the  allocation  of  funds  for
Pension  to  Meritorious  Sportspersons  has  been  increased  from  4  crores  to  10  crores  in  the  financial
year  2025-26  due  to  the  increase  in  the  eligible  application  received  under  the  scheme.The
increased  allocation  in  BE  for  the  FY   2026 -27  is  for  the  reason  that  mega  event  such  as  Asian
Games  and  Commonwealth  Games  are  scheduled  to  be  held  in  2026.  Therefore,  it  is  expected  that
there will be morenumber  of applications under the scheme.
6 .1. 7 The  Committee  take  note  of  the  reduction  of  allocation  from  ₹ 37.00  crore  at  BE
2025 – 26  to  ₹ 28.00  crore  at  RE  stage,  primarily  due  to  a  lower  number  of  eligible  applications,
and  the  subsequent  increase  to  ₹ 40.00  crore  at  BE  2026 – 27  in  anticipation  of  mega  sporting
events  such  as  the  Asian  Games  and  Commonwealth  Games  in  2026.  While  appreciating  the
performance-linked  and  flexible  character  of  the  scheme,  the  Committee  recommend  that  the
Ministry  may  maintain  a  contingency  buffer  within  the  overall  allocation  to  ensure  that
genuine  claims  are  not  constrained  by  budgetary  ceilings  in  years  of  exceptional  sporting
success.  The  Ministry  may  institute  a  robust  forecasting  mechanism  based  on  upcoming
international  sporting  calendars,  historical  performance  trends,  and  athlete  participation  data

51
so  as  to  project  fund  requirements  more  realistically  and  minimize  sharp  variations  at  the
Revised Estimate stage.
6 .1. 8 The  Committee  also  notes  that  although  ₹ 46.00  crore  was  projected  for  2026 – 27,  only
₹ 40.00  crore  has  been  approved  at  the  BE  stage,  reflecting  partial  rationalization.  Given  the
importance  of  timely  and  credible  disbursement  of  incentives  to  maintain  athlete  morale,  the
Committee  recommend s  that  the  Ministry  may  closely  monitor  the  pace  of  applications  and,  if
required,  seek  supplementary  grants  in  a  timely  manner  so  that  no  eligible  sportsperson  or
coach faces delay in receipt of due incentives.
6 .1. 9 The  Committee  also  note  the  substantial  increase  in  allocation  for  Pension  to
Meritorious  Sportspersons  from  ₹ 4.00  crore  to  ₹ 10.00  crore  in  2025 – 26  owing  to  an  increase
in  eligible  beneficiaries.  Considering  the  long-term  welfare  dimension  of  scheme , the
Committee  recommend s  that  the  Ministry  should  periodically  review  pension  rates  and
eligibility  criteria  to  ensure  adequacy  and  sustainability  along  with  strengthening  digital
processing  and  verification  systems  to  have  a  transparent  and  expedit ious  disburs ement
mechanism under all sub-components of the Incentives to Sports Persons Scheme.
6 .2  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National Welfare Program for Sportspersons
6 .2.1 This  welfare-oriented  scheme  provides  financial  assistance  to  former  sportspersons  who
have  represented  the  country  at  international  levels  and  are  facing  financial  hardship,  medical
emergencies,  or  other  distress  conditions.  The  objective  is  to  ensure  dignity  and  social  security  for
athletes who have contributed to national pride.
6 .2.2 The  scheme  embodies  the  principle  of  long-term  athlete  welfare  and  recognizes  the  need  for
post-retirement  support  mechanisms.  It  includes  provisions  for  medical  treatment,  lump-sum
assistance,  and  pensionary  support  under  specific  conditions,  thereby  addressing  gaps  in  social
protection.
6 .2.3 Under  the  Scheme,  the  incentives  given  to  sportspersons  depends  upon  the  number  of
applications  received  in  a  particular  year.  It  intends  to  provide  financial  assistance  to  (i)
sportspersons  now  living  in  indigent  sports  persons;  (ii)  sportspersons  injured  during  the  period  of
their  training  for  competitions  and  also  during  the  competitions,  depending  on  the  nature  of  the
injury;  (iii)  sportspersons  who  bring  glory  to  the  country  in  international  field  and  who  are  disabled
as  an  after  effect  of  their  strenuous  training  or  otherwise  and  to  provide  them  assistance  for  medical
treatment;  (iv)  promote  the  welfare  of  the  sports  persons  generally  in  order  to  alleviate  distress
among  them  and  their  dependents  inindigent  circumstances;  and  (v)  active  sportspersons
individually orcollectively  as a group.
6 .2. 4 The  scheme  shows  consistent  yet  modest  allocations.  Actual  expenditure  in  2024 – 25  was
₹ 0.95  crore,  with  BE  2025 – 26  at  ₹ 2.00  crore  and  RE  reduced  to  ₹ 1.00  crore.  The  BE  2026 – 27  is
maintained at ₹ 2.00 crore, reflecting a 100% increase over RE 2025 – 26.
6 .2. 5 The  Committee  note s  that  the  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National  Welfare  Fund  for
Sportspersons  is  a  vital  welfare-oriented  intervention  aimed  at  providing  financial  assistance

52
to  former  and  active  sportspersons  facing  indigent  circumstances,  medical  emergencies,
disability,  or  distress  conditions.  While  observing  that  the  scheme  is  demand-driven  and  that
the  allocation  of  ₹ 2.00  crore  at  BE  2025 – 26  and  BE  2026 – 27  is  considered  adequate  by  the
Ministry,  the  Committee  further  note s  that  the  AE  in  2024 – 25  stood  at  ₹ 0.95  crore  and  the  RE
2025 – 26  was  reduced  to  ₹ 1.00  crore.  T he  Committee  recommends  that  the  Ministry  should
undertake  wider  outreach  and  awareness  initiatives  in  coordination  with  National  Sports
Federations  and  State  Governments  to  ensure  that  eligible  beneficiaries  are  adequately
informed about the scheme and are able to access timely assistance.
6 .2. 6 The  Committee  observe s  that  the  scheme  addresses  critical  aspects  of  post-retirement
security,  medical  support,  and  social  dignity  of  sportspersons  who  have  brought  laurels  to  the
nation.  Considering  the  welfare  and  humanitarian  character  of  the  Fund,  the  Committee
recommends  that  the  Ministry  should  periodically  review  the  quantum  of  assistance,
eligibility  norms,  and  processing  timelines  to  enhance  accountability , and  also  develop  a
technology-enabled  monitoring  system  to  ensure  transparency,  expeditious  disposal  of  cases,
and optimal utilization of allocated funds.
6 .3  National Sports Development Fund (NSDF)
6 .3.1 The  National  Sports  Development  Fund  (NSDF)  was  established  with  a  view  to  bring  in
adequate  facilities  for  promotion  of  sports  and  games  through  various  sources.   The  purpose  of  the
Fund  is  to  impart  momentum  and  flexibility  in  assisting  towards  the  cause  of  sports.NSDF
recognizes  institutions  and  individuals  as  equal  partners  of  the  Government  in  the  promotion  and
management  of  sports  in  the  country.   It  constitutes  an  important  initiative  for  enabling  institutions
and  public  at  large  to  contribute  to  sports  related  endeavors,  for  forging  meaningful  inter-
institutional  partnerships,  for  mobilizing  extra  budgetary  resources  for  sports  and  for  highlighting
awareness  that  sports  constitute  a  crucial  input  to  the  overall  process  of  development  of  a  person.
Thus,  NSDF  mobilizes  resources  from  the  Government  as  well  as  non-government  organizations
and  individuals  to  provide  the  required  support  for  promotion  of  specific  sports  disciplines  and
improving performance of Indian sports in major international events. 
6 .3. 2 The  Fund  accepts  contributions  from  statutory  bodies  established  under  Acts  of  Parliament
or  State  Legislatures,  public  and  private  sector  enterprises,  trusts,  societies,  and  individuals.  NSDF
also  receives  endowments,  donations  and  other  contributions  for  specific  purposes,  provided  that
such  purposes  are  consistent  with  and  do  not  impede  the  objectives  of  the  Scheme.  NSDF  allows
donors/  contributors  to  indicate  any  specific  purpose  for  which  they  want  their  contributing  to  be
used. 
6 .3. 3 NSDF  supports  sportspersons  by  facilitating  access  to  training  under  coaches  of
international  repute,  providing  technical,  scientific,  and  psychological  support,  and  enabling
exposure  to  international  competitions.  The  Fund  also  provides  financial  assistance  for  the
development  of  sports  infrastructure  and  for  undertaking  activities  aimed  at  the  promotion  and
advancement  of  sports.  The  funds  are  utilized  for  providing  grants  for  sports  development  and
extending  financial  assistance  to  sportspersons,  releasing  block  grants  to  the  Sports  Authority  of
India  for  athletes  under  the  Target  Olympic  Podium  Scheme  (TOPS),  Sportspersons,  Sports

53
Organization,  Academies  etc.  and  meeting  the  establishment  and  administrative  expenditure  of  the
NSDF Secretariat.
6 .3.4 During  the  last  three  financial  years  (FY  2023-24  to  FY  2025-26,  up  to  31.12.2025),  NSDF
released  ₹ 20.16  crore  in  2023-24,  ₹ 20.78  crore  in  2024-25  and  ₹ 18.00  crore  in  2025-26,
aggregating  to  ₹ 58.94  crore  as  block  grants  to  SAI  under  TOPS  for  preparation  of  athletes  for
Olympics,  Paralympics  and  other  major  international  competitions.  This  assistance  supported
training  camps,  international  exposure,  coaching,  sports  science  support  and  procurement  of  high-
performance  equipment,  indirectly  benefiting  a  large  number  of  elite  sportspersons  across
disciplines .
6 .3. 5 Under  the  “ National  Sports  Development  Fund ” , the  NSDF  recorded  Actual  Expenditure  of
₹ 13.71  crore  in  2024 – 25,  with  BE  2025 – 26  at  ₹ 18.00  crore  and  RE  reduced  to  ₹ 12.00  crore.  For
2026 – 27,  ₹ 15.00  crore  has  been  approved  against  projections  of  ₹ 18.00  crore.  This  reflects  a  25%
increase over RE 2025 – 26 but a 16.67% reduction from projections and BE 2025 – 26.
6 .3. 6 The  Government  contribution  to  NSDF  is  25%  of  the  funds  received  from  various  PSUs  /
Corporates  under  their  CSR  funds.  During  the  year  2025-26,  the  total  CSR  funds  received  is  Rs.
17.91  crore.  Hence,  there  was  reduction  at  RE  stage.  The  government  contribution  upto  31st
January, 2026 has been Rs. 7.27 crore.
6 .3. 7 The  Committee  note s  that  the  National  Sports  Development  Fund  (NSDF)  plays  a n
important  role  in  mobilizing  extra-budgetary  resources  for  sports  promotion  through
contributions  from  Government,  Public  Sector  Undertakings,  corporates  under  CSR,
institutions  and  individuals,  and  in  extending  targeted  assistance  to  sportspersons,  academies
and  the  Sports  Authority  of  India  under  the  Target  Olympic  Podium  Scheme  (TOPS).
However,  the  Committee  observe s  that  allocations  under  the  Fund  have  witnessed
fluctuations,  with  BE  2025 – 26  of  ₹ 18.00  crore  being  reduced  to  ₹ 12.00  crore  at  RE  stage  and
BE  2026 – 27  approved  at  ₹ 15.00  crore  against  projections  of  ₹ 18.00  crore.  T he  Committee
recommend s  that  the  Ministry  should  evolve  a  more  practical  resource  mobilization  strategy,
including  advance  engagement  with  CSR  contributors  and  multi-year  commitment
frameworks,  so  as  to  reduce  flu ct ua tion s  in  allocations  and  ensure  continuity  of  high-
performance preparation of athletes.
6 .3.8 The  Committee  observe s  that  Government  contribution  to  the  NSDF  is  linked  to  25  per
cent  of  funds  received  from  PSUs  and  corporates  under  CSR,  and  that  the  budgetary
reduction s  at  RE  stage  is  attributable  to  lower  CSR  receipts  during  the  year.  T he  Committee
recommend s  that  the  Ministry  should  explore  diversification  of  funding  sources,  including
greater  outreach  to  private  foundations,  philanthropic  institutions  and  high  net-worth
individuals,  and  consider  instituting  incentive  mechanisms  for  long-term  endowments  so  as  to
augment the corpus and enhance financial sustainability of the Fund.
6 .3.9 The  Committee  note s  that  substantial  block  grants  amounting  to  ₹ 58.94  crore  have
been  released  during  the  last  three  financial  years  to  SAI  under  TOPS  for  training,
international  exposure,  sports  science  support  and  procurement  of  high-performance
equipment,  thereby  b enefiting  elite  athletes  across  disciplines.  In  order  to  further  strengthen

54
transparency,  the  Committee  recommend s  that  the  Ministry  should  develop  institute  a
structured  impact  assessment  mechanism  with  performance  indicators,  periodic  public
disclosure  of  utilization  details,  and  measurable  outcome  benchmarks  linked  to  international
results,  so  as  to  ensure  optimal  utilization  of  NSDF  resources  and  greater  accountability  in
sports promotion activities.
6 .4  Assistance to National Sports Federations (NSFs)
6 .4.1 This  scheme  provides  financial  assistance  to  recognized  National  Sports  Federations  for
conducting  national  championships,  training  camps,  international  participation,  equipment
purchase,  and  administrative  activities.  The  assistance  strengthens  federations ’ capacity  to  identify
talent,  provide  training  infrastructure,  and  maintain  international  competitiveness.  Compliance  with
governance norms and performance parameters is mandatory for continued funding.
6 .4. 2 Actual  expenditure  in  2024 – 25  was  ₹ 256.06  crore,  rising  to  BE  2025 – 26  of  ₹ 400.00  crore,
which  remained  unchanged  at  RE  stage.  For  2026 – 27,  BE  stands  at  ₹ 425.00  crore  against
projections  of  ₹ 626.21  crore,  showing  a  6.25%  increase  over  BE  2025 – 26  but  32.13%  lower  than
projections.
6 .4. 3 In  view  of  the  revised  norms  of  assistance  under  the  Assistance  to  National  Sports
Federations  (NSFs)  Scheme,  the  additional  funds  will  be  utilised  for  the  preparation  o f the  major
international  sporting  events,  such  as  Asian  Games  2026,  Para  Asian  Games2026,  Commonwealth
Games  2026  etc.  Further,  the  enhanced  budget  will  also  be  utilized  towards  hosting  of  major
international  events  in  the  country,  including  preparation  for  hosting  the  2030  Commonwealth
Games. 
6 .4.4 The  Committee  observes  that  while  the  allocation  under  the  Scheme  “ Assistance  to
National  Sports  Federations  (NSFs) ”  has  increased  from  ₹ 400.00  crore  in  BE  2025 – 26  to
₹ 425.00  crore  in  BE  2026 – 27,  the  act ual  expenditure  in  p re vious  years  ₹ 272.86  crore  in
2022 – 23,  ₹ 321.58  crore  in  2023 – 24  and  ₹ 256.05  crore  in  2024 – 25  indicates  persistent  under-
utilisation  of  funds.  At  the  same  time,  the  projections  for  2026 – 27  ( ₹ 626.21  crore)  are
substantially  higher  than  the  Budget  Estimates,  reflecting  a  resource  gap  in  the  context  of
preparations  for  major  international  sporting  events  such  as  the  Asian  Games  2026,  Para
Asian  Games  2026  and  Commonwealth  Games  2026,  as  well  as  proposed  hosting
commitments.  The  Committee,  therefore,  recommends  that  a  performance-linked
disbursement  framework  with  quarterly  utilisation  reviews  and  outcome-based  benchmarks
should  be  put  in  place  for  National  Sports  Federations,  so  as  to  ensure  timely  fund  absorption,
improved  accountability,  and  optimal  utilisation  of  enhanced  allocations  towards  sports
development and  international event preparedness.
6 .5  Scheme of Human Resource Development in Sports
6 .5.1 This  scheme  focuses  on  capacity  building  of  coaches,  sports  scientists,  technical  officials,
and  support  personnel.  It  includes  scholarships,  specialized  training  programs,  and  exposure  visits
aimed at upgrading professional competence.

55
6 .5.2 The  development  of  skilled  human  resources  is  critical  for  sustaining  competitive
excellence.  By  investing  in  coaching  education  and  certification,  the  scheme  enhances  the  technical
backbone of sports administration and athlete preparation.
6 .5. 3 A  significant  addition  in  the  revised  scheme  is  the  Publication  Component,  which  now
provides  support  for  publications  and  e-Publications  of  outstanding  works  in  sports  and  games,
journals  and  e-Journals  contributing  to  sports  sciences  and  development  and  web-based  applications
(apps)  with  relevance  to  sports  performance,  training  and  awareness.  This  inclusion  aimed  at
promoting  knowledge  dissemination,  encouraging  research  visibility,  and  creating  accessible
resources for athletes, coaches, and the wider sporting community.
6 .5.4 Under  the  Head  “ Scheme  of  Human  Resource  Development  in  Sports ” , an  allocation  of  Rs
4.00  crore  was  made  for  BE  2025-26  which  was  reduced  to  Rs  2.00  crore  at  the  RE  stage.  For  2026-
27  an  allocation  of  Rs.  5.00  crore  has  been  made.  Although,  the  scheme  shows  an  expansionary
intent , however,  the  Actual  expenditure  was  ₹ 2.65  crore  in  2024 – 25,  with  BE  2025 – 26  at  ₹ 4.00
crore  and  again,  RE  reduced  to  ₹ 2.00  crore.  For  2026 – 27,  BE  is  ₹ 5.00  crore  against  projections  of
₹ 10.00 crore, reflecting a 150% increase over RE and 25% increase over BE 2025 – 26.
6 .5.5 The  Committee  was  informed  that  the  reduction  at  RE  stage  from  Rs  4.00  crore  to  Rs  2.00
crore,  is  due  to  low  utilization  of  fund  at  that  time.  The  utilization  of  fund  under  HRDS  scheme
depends  on  the  number  of  proposals  received.  The  number  of  proposals  received  is  less  than  the
anticipated  proposals.  For  2026-27,  an  allocation  of  Rs.  5.00  crore  has  been  made  due  to  anticipated
increase  in  the  outreach  of  the  scheme  on  account  of  revision  in  the  scheme  document  after  the
expiry  of  the  current  validity  of  the  scheme  i.e.  2025-26.  Further,  as  per  the  recent  trend,  increase  in
the  number  of  applications  is  expected  in  2026-27.  Also,  increase  in  Administrative  expenses  like
Resource Procurement, Advertisement  etc is expected.
6 .5.6 The  Committee  notes  that  the  Scheme  of  Human  Resource  Development  in  Sports
(HRDS)  is  important  for  strengthening  the  technical  and  professional  ecosystem  of  sports
through  capacity  building  of  coaches,  sports  scientists,  technical  officials  and  support
personnel.  While  the  BE  for  2026 – 27  has  been  enhanced  to  ₹ 5.00  crore  from  ₹ 4.00  crore  in  BE
2025 – 26  ( ₹ 2.00  crore  at  RE  sta ge),  the  pattern  of  utilisation  is  not  encouraging,  due  to  fewer
proposals  which  indicates  structural  constraints  in  outreach  and  absorption  capacity.  The
Committee  also  notes  that  despite  the  inclusion  of  new  components  such  as  support  for
publications,  e-publications  and  sports-related  digital  applications,  the  projected  requirement
of  ₹ 10.00  crore  has  not  been  fully  matched  in  the  Budget  Estimates  for  FY  2026-27 . The
Committee,  therefore,  recommends  that  the  Ministry  should  project  its  demand  for  higher
allocations  at  the  RE  Stage.  Besides,  the  Ministry  should  undertake  proactive  awareness  and
outreach  measures  in  coordination  with  National  Sports  Federations,  Sports  Universities  and
training  institutions  to  increase  the  number  and  quality  of  proposals  under  the  Scheme,
including  measures  to  ensure  optimal  utilisation  of  funds  to  strengthen  of  the  human  resource
base in sports.
6 .6  National Centre of Sports Sciences & Research (NCSSR)
6 .6.1 The  NCSSR  initiative  promotes  sports  science,  research,  injury  management,  nutrition,
psychology,  and  performance  analytics.  It  integrates  scientific  support  into  athlete  preparation  and
aligns Indian sports with global best practices.

56
6 .6.2 The  scheme  fosters  collaboration  between  academic  institutions,  medical  experts,  and  sports
bodies.  It  emphasizes  evidence-based  training  and  technological  innovation  to  enhance  performance
and reduce injuries.
6 .6.3 Under  the  Head  “ National  Centre  of  Sports  Science  &  Research ” , Rs.  10.00  crore  had  been
allocated  at  BE  (2025-26)  which  was  substantially  increased  to  Rs.  78.64  crore  at  the  RE  stage.  For
2026-27  an  allocation  of  Rs.  15.00  crore  has  been  made  against  projections  of  ₹ 27.50  crore,
showing  a  sharp  80.93%  decrease  over  RE . The  scheme  witnessed  significant  fluctuations  in
allocations of funds f or 2026 – 27.
6 .6.4 The  Committee  notes  with  concern  the  significant  fluctuations  in  budgetary  allocations
under  the  National  Centre  of  Sports  Sciences  &  Research  (NCSSR),  wherein  the  Budget
Estimate  of  ₹ 10.00  crore  for  2025 – 26  was  substantially  enhanced  to  ₹ 78.64  crore  at  the
Revised  Estimate  stage,  despite  actual  expenditure  being  ₹ 5.00  crore  in  FY  2024 – 25.  While
such  an  increase  may  reflect  major  capital  or  project-based  interventions  aimed  at
strengthening  sports  science  infrastructure,  the  sharp  decline  to  ₹ 15.00  crore  in  BE  2026 – 27
against  projections  of  ₹ 27.50  crore  and  an  80.93  per  cent  decrease  over  RE  indicates  the
absence  of  a  stable  funding  framework.  Given  the  critical  importance  of  scientific  support,
injury  management,  nutrition,  psychology  and  performance  analytics  in  achieving
international  competitiveness,  the  Committee  recommends  that  the  Ministry  should  formulate
outcome-oriented  financial  plan  for  NCSSR  with  clearly  defined  research  priorities,
institutional  partnerships  and  performance  indicators,  so  as  to  ensure  consistency  in  funding,
and constant  integration of sports science into athlete preparation.
6 .7  Khelo India
6 .7.1 Khelo  India  is  a  flagship  program  aimed  at  grassroots  sports  development,  talent
identification,  infrastructure  creation,  and  annual  national-level  competitions.  It  seeks  to  build  a
robust  sporting  ecosystem  across  schools  and  universities.  The  program  provides  scholarships  to
talented  athletes,  develops  sports  infrastructure,  and  conducts  Khelo  India  Games  to  promote
competitive  exposure  at  an  early  age.  It  plays  a  transformative  role  in  broad-basing  sports
participation.
6 .7.2 Schemes  like  Khelo  India  have  proven  highly  effective  in  revitalizing  India ’s  sports  culture
at  the  grassroots  level  and  building  a  framework  for  identifying  and  nurturing  talent.  Also,  under
Khelo  India  Scheme,  this  Ministry  has  approved  344  sports  infrastructure  projects,  notified  1067
Khelo  India  Centres,  35  Khelo  India  State  Centres  of  Excellence  and  accredited  322  sports
academies  across  the  country,  including  rural  and  urban  areas . Further,  as  on  date,  2905  Khelo  India
Athletes  have  been  identified  under  the  scheme  and  are  getting  trained  for  various  national  and
international sports events. 
6 .7.3 The  schemes  of  this  Ministry  are  demand-driven  under  which  proposals  when  received  from
the  State/UT  Governments  and  other  eligible  entities  are  analyzed  in  this  Ministry  and  then
sanctioned  according  to  the  parameters  of  the  Scheme,  subject  to  their  completeness,  technical
feasibility  and  availability  of  funds  under  the  Scheme.  The  details  of  the  sports  infrastructure
projects  approved  and  their  sanctioned  cost,  funds  released  and  their  physical  and  financial
progress,  under  the  Khelo  India  Scheme  and  National  Sports  Development  Funds  (NSDF),  across

57
the  country,  including  in  rural  and  urban  areas,  are  available  in  public  domain  on  the  dashboards  of
the  Ministry  at  https://mdsd.kheloindia.gov.in  and  http://www.nsdf.yas.gov.in/nsdf-glance.html,
respectively.
6 .7.4  The  Committee  also  noted  that  funds  are  released  by  this  Ministry  scheme-wise  and  not  state-
wise.  Further,  the  Khelo  India  Scheme,  which  is  a  flagship  programme  of  the  Department,  is  a
demand-driven  scheme.  The  sports  infrastructure  proposals  received  from  the  State/UT
Governments  and  other  eligible  entities  are  considered  for  financial  support,  subject  to  their
completeness, technical feasibility and availability of funds under the scheme. 
6 .7.5  Under  the  Khelo  India  Centre  (KIC),  vertical  of  Khelo  India  Scheme,  One-Time  Non-
Recurring  Grant  of  Rs.  5  lakh  per  discipline  is  provided  for  preparation/ upgradation  of  sports  field,
purchase  of  sports  equipment,  CCTV  set-up,  etc.   Further,  an  annual  recurring  grant  of  Rs.  5  lakh
are  provided  for  the  remuneration  of  Past  Champion  Athletes  (PCAs),  purchase  of  sports
equipment,  sports  kit,  consumables,  competition/event  participation,  etc.  The  maximum
remuneration  per  PCA  is  Rs.  3  lakh  per  annum.   Also,  the  maximum  amount  allocated  for  purchase
of  sports  equipment,  sports  kits,  consumables,  competition/event  participation,  etc.  is  Rs.  2  lakh
annually.  The  funds  are  allocated/released  by  SAI  HO  through  SAI  Regional  Centres  to  the
States/UT and its utilization and monitoring is done through SAI Regional Centres.
6 .7.6 As  of  now,  22  editions  of  Khelo  India  Games  have  been  organized  viz  07  editions  of  Khelo
India  Youth  Games  (KIYG),  05  editions  of  Khelo  India  Winter  Games  (KIWG),  05  editions  of
Khelo  India  University  Games  (KIUG),   02  Khelo  India  Para  Games  (KIPG)  and  02  Khelo  India
Beach  Games  (KIBG)  and  01  Khelo  India  Water  Sports  Festival  (KIWSF).  These  games  have
witnessed a total participation of more than 61000 athletes.
6 .7.7 Under  Khelo  IndiaTalent  Identification  &  Development  (KITD),  selected  talent  is  identified
as  Khelo  India  Athletes  (KIAs )based  on  the  recommendation  of  the  Talent  Identification  and
Development  Committee  (TIDC).  The  KIAs  training  in  the  Khelo  India  accredited  academies  are
entitled  for  the  financial  support  in  tune  of  Rs.  6.28  Lakhs  per  annum  inclusive  of  Rs.  10,000/-  per
month  as  Out  of  Pocket  Allowance  (OPA).  Total  2905  KIAs  are  being  supported  across  22
disciplines  and  around  322  academies  have  been  accredited  under  Khelo  India  Scheme  in  various
sports disciplines. 
6 .7.8 Khelo  India  Centres  (KICs)  initiative  was  launched  to  maximize  the  utilization  of  existing
sports  infrastructure  across  various  States  and  Union  Territories  (UTs)  and  to  provide  sustainable
sports  training  facilities  for  young  athletes.  Currently,  1067  KICs  have  been  notified.  Khelo  India
State  Centres  of  Excellence  (KISCEs)  are  important  for  upgrading  State/UT  sports  centres  to  global
standards. Currently, 35 KISCEs have been notified across 34 States/UTs.
6 .7.9 Fit  India  Movement  was  launched  by  the  Prime  Minister  of  India  on  August  29,  2019,  with
the  vision  to  promote  healthier  lifestyles  by  encouraging  individuals  and  communities  to
incorporate  physical  activity  and  sports  into  their  daily  routines.  Fit  India  Freedom  Run  5.0 event
saw  participation  from  over  10,433  organizers  and  1.27  crore  individuals,  marking  its  success. Fit
India  Mobile  App  (FIMA),  launched  in  2021,  helps  users  track  their  fitness  parameters  and  provides
personalized  fitness  tips.  The  Fit  India  Cycling  Drive  is  a  national  initiative  launched  on  17th
December 2024 to promote cycling as a means of staying fit and healthy.
6 .7.10 Khelo  India  women ’s  leagues  are  being  conducted  in  34  disciplines  across  the  nation.  Total
2407  competitions  have  been  conducted  with  participation  of  2,80,142  women  athletes. The
Government  provides  financial  assistance  and  support  for  local  and  indigenous  sports  through

58
Khelo  India  Programme  to  promote  national  integration,  peace  and  harmony.  E.g.,  Silambam ,
Kabaddi , Mallakhamb , Kalaripayyattu , Gatka , Thang -Ta  and  Yogasana . To  start  with  indigenous
game league in Gatka  discipline was recently held at Patiala in the month of Jan-2025.
6 .7.11 Since  the  launch  of  the  Khelo  India  Scheme,  the  Government  has  expanded  its  support  for
domestic  sports  by  adding  new  national  competitions.  Initially,  the  Scheme  started  with  only  Khelo
India  Youth  Games  and  University  Game.  Now,  there  are  six  major  events  in  total.  The  new
additions include the Winter Games , Para Games , Beach Games , and the Water Sports Festival.
6 .7. 12 Budget  allocation  under  Khelo  India  forms  a  substantial  share  of  the  sports  budget,
reflecting  its  flagship  status.  Funds  are  utilized  for  scholarships,  infrastructure  grants,  and
competition  management.  Allocation  and  expenditure  under  Khelo  India  Scheme  during  last  three
years are as under:

6 .7. 13 During  the  last  three  financial  years,  a  total  amount  of  ₹ 2,089.31  crore  has  been  released
under  Khelo  India  Scheme.  Further,  55  new  sports  infrastructure  projects  amounting  to  ₹ 679.46
crore  were  approved  during  this  period,  out  of  which  17  projects,  amounting  to  ₹ 384.97  crore,  have
been  completed . However,  c ertain  challenges  were  encountered  due  to  the  revision  in  the  funds
flow  system , but  necessary  assistance  was  extended  to  the  grantee  agencies  to  facilitate  its
implementation.
6 .7. 14 Under  the  scheme  Khelo  India , Actual  expenditure  stood  at  ₹ 620.75  crore  in  2024 – 25 . On
the  other  hand,  allocation  in  BE  2025 – 26  was  ₹ 1000.00  crore  whereas  the  RE  reduced  to  ₹ 700.00
crore.  For  2026 – 27,  BE  is  ₹ 924.35  crore  against  projections  of  ₹ 1200.00  crore.  This  reflects  a
32.05% increase over RE but a 7.57% reduction compared to BE 2025 – 26.
6 .7.1 5 The  Committee  appreciates  that  Khelo  India,  as  the  flagship  programme  of  the
Ministry,  continues  to  command  the  largest  share  of  resources  and  has  made  notable  progress
in  grassroots  sports  development  through  the  approval  of  344  infrastructure  projects,
notification  of  1067  Khelo  India  Centres  (KICs),  35  Khelo  India  State  Centres  of  Excellence
(KISCEs),  accreditation  of  322  academies,  and  identification  of  2905  Khelo  India  Athletes.
However,  the  Committee  observes  a  consistent  gap  between  Budget  Estimates  and  actual
expenditure  during  the  last  three  financial  years,  with  actual  expenditure  in  2024 – 25  at
₹ 620.75  crore  against  BE  of  ₹ 900  crore  and  RE  of  ₹ 746.54  crore,  and  similar  moderation
visible  in  2025 – 26  and  2026 – 27  allocations  vis-à-vis  utilisation  pattern . The  Committee,
therefore,  is  of  the  view  that  the  advance  planning  and  phased  fund  release  mechanisms

59
should  be  adopted  in  consultation  with  States/UTs  to  minimize  surrender  of  funds  and  ensure
timely execution of sanctioned infrastructure and competition-related activities.
6 .7.1 6 The  Committee  further  observes  that  while  55  infrastructure  projects  amounting  to
₹ 679.46  crore  were  approved  during  the  last  three  years,  only  17  projects  ( ₹ 384.97  crore)  have
been  completed,  indicating  implementation  bottlenecks.  Given  that  the  Scheme  is  demand-
driven  and  funds  are  released  scheme-wise  rather  than  state-wise,  disparities  in  project
readiness  may  affect  uniform  sports  development.  The  Committee  recommends  that  a  project
monitoring  dashboard  with  tracking  and  third-party  technical  audits  support  to  State
implementing  agencies  be  developed  to  expedite  completion  of  infrastructure  projects  and
ensure optimal utilization of investment s .
6 .7.1 7 The  Committee  appreciates  the  comprehensive  athlete  support  framework  under  the
Khelo  India  Talent  Identification  &  Development  (KITD)  vertical,  including  financial
assistance  of  ₹ 6.28  lakh  per  annum  per  athlete  and  expansion  of  multiple  editions  of  the  Khelo
India  Games  with  participation  of  over  61,000  athletes.  However,  to  ensure  constant  high
performance  and  measurable  outcomes,  the  Committee  recommends  that  periodic
performance  evaluation  of  Khelo  India  Athletes  and  accredited  academies  be  undertaken
through  clearly  defined  benchmarks  linked  to  national  and  international  medal  prospects,
with  better  coordination  between  Khelo  India  Centres,  State  Centres  of  Excellence  and
National Coaching Camps.
6 .7. 18 The  Committee  also  notes  the  integration  of  allied  initiatives  such  as  women ’s  leagues
across  34  disciplines,  promotion  of  indigenous  sports,  and  alignment  with  mass  participation
movements  like  Fit  India.  While  these  initiatives  contribute  to  social  inclusion,  gender  equity
and  community  engagement,  the  Committee  recommends  that  a n  impact  assessment
framework  taking  into  account  participation  metrics,  and  socio-economic  outreach  indicators
should  be  developed , so  as  to  evaluate  long-term  outcomes  and  monitor  future  budgetary
allocations should be developed  under the Scheme.
6 .8  E nhancement of Sports Facility in  J&K
6 .8.1 This  initiative  focuses  on  strengthening  sports  infrastructure  in  Jammu  &  Kashmir  to
promote  youth  engagement  and  national  integration.  It  includes  construction,  renovation,  and
modernization  of  stadiums  and  training  centers.  The  scheme  aims  to  channel  youth  energy  into
constructive  sporting  activities  and  create  opportunities  for  talent  from  the  region  to  participate  in
national and international competitions.
6 .8.2 Budget  allocations  are  targeted  and  project-based,  requiring  timely  fund  release  and  strict
monitoring  to  avoid  cost  overruns.   The  scheme  shows  project-based  variability  and  there  was  no
expenditure  in  2024 – 25 . However,  the  BE  2025 – 26  was  ₹ 20.00  crore  but  RE  reduced  to  ₹ 2.00
crore.  For  2026 – 27,  BE  stands  at  ₹ 5.00  crore  against  projections  of  ₹ 8.00  crore,  reflecting  150  per
cent  increase over RE but 75  per cent  decline over BE 2025 – 26.
6 .8.3 The  Committee  not es  that  the  Scheme  for  Enhancement  of  Sports  Facility  in  Jammu  &
Kashmir  has  exhibited  considerable  budgetary  fluctuation,  with  no  expenditure  incurred  in
FY  2024 – 25 . On  the  other  hand,  the  Budget  Estimate  of  ₹ 20.00  crore  for  FY  2025 – 26  reduced
to  ₹ 2.00  crore  only  at  the  Revised  Estimate  stage,  and  the  allocation  for  FY  2026 – 27  fixed  at
₹ 5.00  crore  against  projections  of  ₹ 8.00  crore.  Such  wide  variations  may  delay  in  project
finalization  and  implementation,  or  procedural  constraints  in  fund  release.  Considering  the

60
strategic  importance  of  strengthening  sports  infrastructure  in  Jammu  &  Kashmir  for  youth
engagement  and  integration  of  regional  talent  into  national  competitions,  the  Committee
recommends  that  a  time-bound,  project-specific  implementation  roadmap  with  clear
milestones,  streamlined  approval  processes  and  quarterly  monitoring  mechanisms  should  be
prepared , so  as  to  ensure  timely  execution  of  sanctioned  works  and  avoid  cost  overruns  to
achieve intended sporting outcomes in the region.
6 .9  Promotion of Sports Goods Manufacturing
6 .9.1 This  initiative  promotes  domestic  manufacturing  of  sports  equipment  to  support  the  “ Make
in  India ”  vision  and  reduce  dependency  on  imports.  It  encourages  quality  standardization  and
export competitiveness.
6 .9.2 The  scheme  supports  MSMEs,  innovation  in  sports  technology,  and  development  of  clusters
for sports goods manufacturing, particularly in traditional hubs.
6 .9.3 Budgetary  provisions  are  focused  on  capacity  building,  quality  certification,  and
promotional  activities.  Enhanced  financial  incentives  could  further  strengthen  global
competitiveness.
6 .9.4 This  is  a  newly  introduced  major  allocation  with  BE  2026 – 27  at  ₹ 500.00  crore,  with  no
prior  allocations  reflected  in  earlier  years.  The  introduction  signifies  strategic  diversification  toward
industrial promotion within the sports ecosystem.
6 .9.5 The  substantial  outlay  indicates  policy  prioritization,  likely  aligned  with  domestic
manufacturing,  export  promotion,  and  self-reliance  objectives.  As  a  new  head,  effective
implementation frameworks will be critical.
6 .9.6 General  industrial  zones  often  lack  the  required  testing  labs  required  for  sports
equipment.  The  Committee  is  of  the  view  that  manufacturing  centres  in  exiting  industrial
hubs,  already  equipped  with  common  facilities  for  carbon  fiber  molding,  wood  seasoning,  and
synthetic  leather  processing  may  be  developed  for  successful  implementation  of  sports  goods
manufacturing schools.
6 .10 Scheme  of  Cash  Incentive  to  Medal  Winners  in  International  Sports  Events  and  their
Coaches
Introduced  in  1986,  the  'Cash  Incentive  to  Medal  Winners  in  International  Sports  Events  and  their
Coaches'  scheme  aims  to  encourage  and  motivate  outstanding  sportspersons  to  strive  for  higher
achievements  while  attracting  the  younger  generation  to  pursue  sports  as  a  career.  Through  this
initiative,  cash  awards  ranging  from  ₹ 1.00  lakh  to  ₹ 75.00  lakh  are  conferred  upon  medal-winning
sportspersons  and  their  respective  coaches  in  recognition  of  their  accomplishments  at  major
international  sporting  events.  This  financial  incentive  serves  as  a  means  to  reward  exceptional
performances and foster an environment conducive to sporting excellence.
National Sports Awards (NSAs)
6 .10.1 The  details  of  National  Sports  Awards  viz.  Rajiv  Gandhi  Khel  Ratna  Award  changed  to
Major  Dhyan  Chand  Award,  Arjuna  Award,  Dronacharya  Award  (Regular),  Dronacharya

61
(Lifetime),  Dhyanchand  Awards  honouring  them  for  their  sporting  achievements  and  contribution
as sportsperson and coaches, are given below:
• Major  Dhyan  Chand  Khel  Ratna  Award  (earlier  known  as  Rajiv  Gandhi  Khel  Ratna
Award)  is  the  highest  individual  sports  award,  given  to  sportsperson(s)  in  recognition  of
spectacular and most outstanding performance in sports over the last four years.
• Arjuna  Award  is  conferred  on  sportspersons  to  recognize  their  outstanding
achievements  at  the  international  level.  Arjuna  Award  (Lifetime)  is  awarded  to
sportspersons  who  have  contributed  to  sports  through  their  performance  and  continue  to
promote sports even after retirement from active sporting careers.
• Dronacharya  Award  is  presented  to  honor  coaches  who  have  consistently  demonstrated
outstanding  and  meritorious  work,  enabled  sportspersons  to  excel  in  international
events,  and  motivated  them  to  achieve  the  highest  standards  of  performance,  thereby
bringing glory to the country.
• Rashtriya  Khel  ProtsahanPuraskar  (RKPP)  is  awarded  to  corporate  entities,  public  and
private  organizations,  Sports  Control  Boards,  and  NGOs  for  their  significant
contribution to the promotion and development of sports.
• Maulana  Abul  Kalam  Azad  (MAKA)  Trophy  is  awarded  to  the  overall  winner  of  the
Khelo India University Games.
• Dhyan  Chand  Awards  for  Lifetime  Achievement  in  Sports  and  Games  was
discontinued from 2024 onwards.

6 .10.3 The  Committee  may  recommend  that  the  Department  of  Sports  ensure  timely
declaration  of  National  Sports  Awards  every  year  through  a  fixed  annual  calendar.  Further,
the  selection  criteria,  evaluation  parameters  and  marks  awarded  by  the  selection  committee
may  be  placed  in  the  public  domain  after  the  awards  are  announced.  This  would  enhance
transparency,  credibility  and  public  confidence  in  the  award  selection  process  while  ensuring
that deserving sportspersons and coaches are recognized without undue delay.
6 .10.4 The  Committee  may  also  recommend  that  the  Government  periodically  revise  the
quantum  of  cash  incentives  and  pension  provided  under  the  Scheme  of  Cash  Incentive  to
Medal  Winners  and  the  Sports  Fund  for  Pension  to  Meritorious  Sportspersons,  taking  into
account  inflation  and  rising  training  costs.  The  scope  of  the  pension  scheme  may  also  be
expanded  to  cover  a  larger  number  of  deserving  retired  sportspersons,  particularly  those
from  less-popular  sports  disciplines,  to  ensure  financial  security  and  encourage  greater
participation in sports across the country.

6 .12 Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National Welfare Program for Sportspersons
6 .12.1 The  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National  Welfare  Program  for  Sportspersons  (formerly
known  as  the  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National  Welfare  Fund  for  Sportspersons  - PDUNWFS)
was  started  in  March  1982  with  the  aim  of  supporting  outstanding  former  sportspersons  who  had
brought  glory  to  the  nation  through  their  achievements  but  are  currently  facing  financial  hardships.
This  scheme  provides  assistance  ranging  up  to  ₹ 10  lakhs  to  eligible  individuals  on  various  grounds,
including  medical  treatment,  procurement  of  equipment,  and  participation  in  national  and
international  sporting  events.  By  extending  this  support,  the  program  ensures  that  the  contributions
of  these  eminent  sportspersons  are  recognized  and  their  welfare  is  safeguarded  during  time  of  need.
The  objective  of  the  Scheme  of  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National  Welfare  Program  for
Sportspersons  is  providing  financial  assistance  to  outstanding  sportspersons/family  members  living
in  indigent  circumstances  for  various  purposes  like  ameliorate  condition,  medical  treatment,
injuries, procurement of sports equipment ’s etc.

providing  lump-sum  financial  assistance  to  sportspersons  for  the  last  five  years  (up  to  31/12/2025)
are given below:

6 .13 Human Resource Development in Sports
In  the  financial  year  2013-14,  the  Department  of  Sports  launched  the  Human  Resource
Development  in  Sports  Scheme,  a  Central  Sector  Scheme,  after  a  comprehensive  revision  of  the
previous  "Scheme  of  Talent  Search  and  Training."  The  primary  objective  of  this  new  scheme  is  to
emphasize  the  academic  and  intellectual  aspects  of  sports  management  by  awarding  fellowships  to
deserving  candidates  for  specialized  studies  at  the  Master's  and  Doctoral  levels  in  specific  sports
and  games  disciplines  where  there  is  a  shortage  of  human  resources.  By  fostering  advanced
education  and  research  in  these  areas,  the  scheme  aims  to  develop  a  skilled  workforce  capable  of
effectively managing and promoting sports in the country.
6 .13.1 NATIONAL CENTRES OF EXCELLENCE (NCOE)
Selection  is  done  by  Talent  Identification  and  Development  Committees  (TIDC)  of  all  the
discipline. TIDC is empowered to select/ weed out athletes from NCOEs.
6 .13.2 SAI TRAINING CENTRES (STC) AND EXETNSION CENTRES OF STCS
Criteria for Induction:
(a)  Individual  Events  (Age:  12  to  18  years)  :Up  to  eighth  (08)  place  in  Sub-Junior  (including
Cadet)  and  Junior  National  Championships  o rganized  by  a  recognized  National  Sports
Federation  and  upto  sixth  (06)  place  in  Inte r-University  Championships  conducted  by
Association  of  Indian  Universities,  and  School  Games  Federation  of  India  during  the  current  or
the year preceding admission.
OR
Sports  persons  who  obtain  first  three  (03)  places  in  the  State  Championship  Conducted  by  the
recognized State Sports Association.
OR
Sports  persons  who  secure  any  of  the  first  three  (03)  positions  in  the  North  East  Games  &
National Rural and W omen Championships.
OR
Sports  person  who  has  represented  India  in  any  recognized  Championship/  Tournament  by  the
recognized international federation concerned.
OR
The  first  three  (03)  position-holders  of  District  Championships,  Inter-Education  District  Level
small  Competition,  Championships  held  by  Confederation  of  Public  Schools,  CBSE,  Kendriya
Vidyalaya , Navodaya  Vidyalaya , etc.;  may  be  considered  for  participation  in  the  selection
trials.
b) Team  Events  (Age:  10  to  18years)  :The  talent  who  could  not  reach  the  minimum  level  of
motor  quality  as  per  battery  of  tests  may  be  provisionally  selected  for  six  months  and  only  after
passing  the  motor  quality  tests  and  specific  skill  test  subsequently,  formal  induction  may  be
made, if found fit. 

65
Training  diary  of  each  individual  trainee  must  be  maintained,  which  will  be  taken  into
consideration at the time of retention and weeding out process.
Performance  criteria  for  induction:  Any  member  of  a  team  which  has  obtained  first  four  (4)
positions  in  Sub-Junior  and  Junior  National  Championships  o rganized  by  recognized  National
Sports  Federation  and  first  two  (02)  position  holder  in  Inte r-Zonal  and  Inte r-University
Championships  conducted  by  Association  of  Indian  Universities  and  School  Games  Federation
of India.
OR
Member  of  a  team  which  obtained  first  (01)  or  second  (02)  position  in  the  State  Championship
o rganized by a recognized State Sports Association.
OR
Sports  person  who  has  represented  India  as  a  member  of  the  Sub-Junior  and  Junior  team  in  any
recognized Championship/ Tournament for which team was officially sent by the Govt. of India.
OR
Member of W inner and Runners-up in team games in the North East Games.
OR
Sports  persons  who  have  participated  in  the  recognized  State  level  competitions  o rganized  by
State  Sports  Associations,  State  Sports  Council,  and  State  Sports  Departments  may  be
considered for participation in the selection trials.
6 .13.3 P r e-condition  for  Admission:  The  admission  in  the  above  two  categories  may  be
made  based  on  the  performance  indicators,  Anthropometric  measurement,  Physiological  &
Psychological  tests  and  depending  on  age,  discipline,  event  and  evaluation  of  future  potential
by  appearing  in  the  selection  Tests.  There  will  be  no  direct  admission.  The  admission  will  be
only  on  the  basis  of  performance  and  battery  of  test  result  and  are  to  be  documented  at  the  time
of induction.
Lateral  Entry:  Those  who  achieved  the  desired  performance  in  the  District,  State,  National
and  International  level  competitions  and  successfully  completed  the  battery  of  tests,  technical
and specific skill tests may be inducted at any time of the year.
Retention  Criteria:  Retention  of  the  athlete  is  based  on  his/her  maintaining  the  minimum
level  of  performance  on  the  basis  of  which  he/she  was  admitted  and  also  achieving  the  ta rget
set  for  the  yea r. Battery  of  test  result,  specific  test  result  and  performance  appraisal  record
considered  at  the  time  of  admission  should  be  properly  documented  as  base  performance  so  as
to compare the performance enhancement of the trainees periodically. 
Training  diary  of  each  individual  trainee  is  maintained,  which  is  taken  into  consideration  at  the
time of retention and weeding out.    
Medical  Checkup,  and  Age  Verification  is  essential  especially  when  admission  is  done  on  the
basis  of  performance  in  the  Sub-Junior  and  Junior  level  Competitions  as  an  e ffective
preventive measure against age fraud.

66
W eeding out:
a) Not maintaining the expected level of performances.
b) Injury in capacitating for more than six months from training and or competition; and
c) Dope abuse, age fraud, misconduct etc.
Monitoring, Half Y early Scientific Assessment & Academic backup for trainees admitted:
a) Close  monitoring  and  half  yearly  scientific  assessment  of  all  trainees  admitted  is  done  by
the  Institutional/Regional  Heads  by  engaging  the  services  of  in-house  sports  science
facilities or through renowned Sports Science Institutions.
b) As far as possible, e fforts are made admission in nearby schools;
c) Induction  of  talent  may  be  a  continuous  process  instead  of  linking  with  Academic  session
so  as  to  enable  SAI  to  admit  the  talent  whenever  a  talent  is  spotted  and  found  eligible  for
admission
Relaxation  in  the  retention  of  trainees  beyond  the  age  of  18  years  of  age  and  upto  21  years
can  be  considered  by  the  Head  of  Academic  Institutions/Regions  in  special  cases  only
where there is strong justification based on performance and future prospects.
6 .13.4 NATIONAL SPORTS TALENT CONTEST (NSTC) SCHEME
REGULAR  SCHOOLS:  The  performance  of  the  athletes  in  the  battery  of  tests  and  achievements
at the time of induction should be clearly documented. 
Age : 8 to14 years
Individual/ Team Events:
a.  Athletes,  who  are  medal  winners  in  State/National  Level  Competitions  are  admitted  into  the
scheme, subject, to their being found medically fit.
b. Athletes  who  are  medal  winners  at  District  Level  Competition  or  have  participation  in  State
Level  Competitions  are  admitted  subject  to  their  being  found  fit  medically  and  physically  and
also have the required potential which is assessed by battery of tests.
c.  For  selection  from  the  remote,  tribal  &  coastal  areas,  the  athletes  are  also  selected  by  o rganizing
competitions  among  participants.  Selection  is  done  by  a  selection  committee  consisting  of
representatives  of  SAI,  School/  Akharas , SAI  coaches,  sports  scientists  etc.  The  sports  persons
identified  on  this  basis  are  o ffered  admission  after  age  verification,  medical  examination  and  on
found suitable by applying battery of tests.
P r e-condition  for  Admission : The  admission  in  the  above  two  categories  may  be  made  based  on
the  performance  indicators,  Anthropometric  measurement,  Physiological  &  Psychological  tests  and
depending  on  age,  discipline,  event  and  evaluation  of  future  potential  and  battery  of  test  result  and
are to be documented at the time of induction.
Retention  Criteria : Retention  of  the  athletes  will  be  based  on  his/her  maintaining  the  minimum
level  of  performance  on  the  basis  of  which  the  inmate  was  admitted  and  also  achieving  the  ta rget  set
for the yea r.

67
W eeding out:
a)  Not maintaining the expected level of performance.
b)  Dope abuse, age fraud, misconduct.
Monitoring,  Half  Yearly  Scientific  Assessment  &  Academic  back  up  for  athletes  admitted : It is
recommended  that  close  monitoring  and  half  yearly  scientific  assessment  of  all  athletes  admitted
may  be  done  by  Institutional/Regional  Heads  by  engaging  the  services  of  in-house  sports  science
facilities or by engaging the services of renowned Sports Science Institutions.
Relaxation:  However,  relaxation  both  for  lower  and  upper  age  limit  as  well  as  induction  can  be
granted  in  exceptional  cases  based  on  outstanding  performance  in  Battery  of  Tests  and  also  taking
into account specific nature of sports, for which prior approval of DG, SAI would be needed.
IGMAs:  The  performance  of  the  athletes  in  the  battery  of  tests  and  achievements  at  the  time  of
induction should be clearly documented. 
Selection Criteria for Induction:
a. Talents,  who  are  medal  winners  in  State/National  Level  Competitions  are  admitted  into  the
scheme, subject, to their being found medically fit.
b. Talents  who  are  medal  winners  at  District  Level  Competition  or  have  participation  in  State
Level Competitions are admitted subject to their being found fit medically and physically.
c. The  scouting  of  the  talent  in  indigenous  games  is  to  be  done  on  the  basis  of  open  competitions
among  participants.  Selection  is  done  by  a  selection  committee  consisting  of  representatives  of
SAI,  Institutions,  SAI  coaches,  guru/mentor  of  the  concerned  game.  The  sports  persons
identified on this basis are o ffered admission after age verification, medical examination etc.
6 .13.5  The  Committee  notes  that  the  Scheme  of  Cash  Incentive  to  Medal  Winners  in
International  Sports  Events  and  their  Coaches  has  played  a  vital  role  in  rewarding  excellence
and  motivating  sportspersons  to  achieve  higher  standards  at  global  platforms.  However,  in
view  of  the  evolving  competitive  landscape  and  rising  performance  benchmarks,  the
Committee  recommends  that  a  comprehensive  review  of  the  incentive  structure  should  be
undertaken  to  ensure  timely  disbursement,  rationalization  of  award  slabs  in  line  with  inflation
and  international  standards,  and  greater  transparency  in  eligibility  criteria,  particularly  with
regard  to  coaches  and  support  staff  who  contribute  significantly  to  medal-winning
performances.
6 .13.6 The  Committee  further  observes  that  while  the  National  Sports  Awards  continue  to
recognize  outstanding  sportspersons  and  coaches  under  various  categories,  fluctuations  in  the
number  of  awardees  and  the  discontinuation  or  restructuring  of  certain  awards  call  for
greater  clarity  and  consistency.  The  Committee  recommends  that  the  Ministry  establish  a
stable,  well-publicized  selection  calendar  with  clearly  defined  evaluation  parameters,  digital
nomination  processes,  and  wider  stakeholder  consultation  so  as  to  preserve  the  prestige  of  the
Awards and enhance their credibility and outreach.
6 .13.7 With  regard  to  the  Scheme  of  Sports  Fund  for  Pension  to  Meritorious  Sportspersons,
the  Committee  notes  a  declining  trend  in  allocation , and  number  of  beneficiaries  over  the  last

68
five  years  despite  the  Scheme ’s  objective  of  providing  assured  post-retirement  financial
security.  The  Committee  recommends  to  review  eligibility  norms  and  pension  rates
periodically,  expand  the  beneficiary  base  where  warranted,  and  ensure  automatic  revision
mechanisms  linked  to  cost-of-living  criteria  so  that  former  sportspersons  who  have  brought
laurels to the nation are not left in financial distress.
6 .13.8 The  Committee  also  notes  that  under  the  Pandit  Deendayal  Upadhyay  National
Welfare  Program  for  Sportspersons,  allocations  have  remained  constant  at  ₹ 2.00  crore
annually,  while  expenditure  has  shown  a  gradual  decline  in  recent  years.  Considering  that  the
Scheme  is  intended  to  provide  urgent  financial  assistance  for  medical  treatment,  injuries  and
other  exigencies  of  sports  persons/  their  family  members , the  Committee  recommends  that  to
strengthen  awareness  among  eligible  beneficiaries,  simplify  application  procedures  through
an  online  portal,  and  create  a  fast-track  mechanism  for  medical  emergencies  to  ensure  that
deserving  sportspersons  receive  timely  support.  Further,  the  Committee  also  recommends
that  necessary  steps  should  be  taken  for  continuation  of  this  scheme  during  the  next  Financial
Cycle ( i.e.  2026-2031)
6 .13.9 The  Committee  further  observes  that  the  various  talent  development  and  training
schemes  implemented  through  SAI  institutions — including  National  Centres  of  Excellence
(NCOEs),  SAI  Training  Centres  (STCs),  NSTC  and  Indigenous  Games  initiatives — have
detailed  induction,  retention  and  monitoring  criteria  based  on  scientific  assessment  and
performance  benchmarks.  While  these  frameworks  are  comprehensive,  the  Committee
recommends  that  a  unified  digital  athlete  management  system  be  developed  to  integrate
performance  data,  scientific  assessments,  training  diaries,  medical  records  and  academic
support  details  across  all  centres , thereby  enabling  real-time  monitoring,  minimizing
duplication,  preventing  age  fraud  and  doping-related  irregularities,  and  ensuring  a
transparent and merit-based development  from grassroots to elite levels.
7 .1 AUTONOMOUS BODIES UNDER THE DEPARTMENT OF SPORTS
7 .1.1 As  modern  sports  have  become  highly  competitive,  the  use  of  advanced  infrastructure,
equipment,  and  scientific  support  has  transformed  the  international  sporting  landscape.  Recognizing
the  growing  demands  for  such  state-of-the-art  resources,  the  Government  of  India  has  also
undertaken  several  initiatives  to  assist  athletes  by  providing  training,  exposure  to  international
competitions,  and  access  to  scientific  backing  and  cutting-edge  equipment.  This  comprehensive
support aims to equip Indian sportspersons with the tools necessary to excel on the world stage.
7 .1 .2 There are five Autonomous bodies under the Department of Sports, namely:
1. Sports Authority of India (SAI)
2. National Anti-Doping Agency (NADA)
3. National Dope Testing Laboratory (NDTL)
4. National Sports University (NSU)
5. Lakshmibai National Institute of Physical Education, Gwalior (LNIPE)
7 .1 .3 The  Autonomous  Body-wise  budget  allocation  (RE)  of  FY  2025-26  and  FY  2026-27  under
Other Autonomous Bodies is as under:
